| 65 | 2026-10-17 | Geometry | FIXED (see #110) | PS2 car geometry preview topology is unknown. `eStripEntry::NumPolys` totals 107,890, unlike the 152,216 triangles from the old triangle-fan guess | The 547 mesh headers with version `0x20` agree with their strip-table counts and VIF data sizes; all 11,246 decoded VIF strip counts agree with table `NumVerts`. Each strip packet begins with a signed V4-8 UNPACK to VU address 0; across all 11,246 strips its four bytes are exactly `(NumVerts, (NumVerts-1)>>2, 7, LightMaterialIndex*4)`, identifying it as strip control metadata rather than an index list. It then writes two V3-32 vectors at VU addresses 2 and 3, consistent with per-strip min/max, plus V4-16 SoA positions from address 5. Mapping each signed 16-bit coordinate as `min + q/32768 * (hi-lo)` keeps all 174,708 decoded vertices inside their parent solid AABBs (2 mm tolerance); denominator 16384 places 1,130 vertices outside and 4096 places 150,817 outside. This strongly supports Q15 normalized positions and rejects `/4096`. The updated point-only `tools/solid_preview.py` decodes all 612 meshes (11,246 strips; 174,708/174,708 vertices); the untransformed aggregate point cloud appears car-shaped, but does not validate topology or subpart transforms. UG2 loader `0x001bf3b8` parses solid chunks and calls `0x001e1880`, which records pointers to `0x134a00/01/02`; these functions do not interpret packet topology. `NumPolys` often differs from `NumVerts-2`; treating the vertex sequence as an alternating triangle strip yields 152,216 candidate triangles. Removing triangles with repeated positions leaves 145,709; also removing 300 additional collinear triangles leaves 145,409, still 37,519 above the 107,890 table polygons. The excess remains in every strip-flag group (`0x4080`: 4,340; `0x4180`: 32,834; `0xc180`: 345), so these flag groups alone do not explain the topology. The matching MW PS2 `eStripEntry` layout confirms the table field offsets. UG2 render paths `0x001d3f78` and `0x001d43f8` read `PolyGroupNumber` as an index into the render-context group table and batch adjacent strips with the same group; the lit path also builds per-strip lighting data. Thus this field controls draw grouping/render state, not vertex topology. These paths enqueue each strip's VIF data address (`mesh VIF base + VifOffset*16`) and `NumVerts` into the DMA chain; separate node builders emit context/state changes (`0x001c7d08`, `0x001c7df8`). Flush routine `0x001d0b88` drains 22 render-context queues and assembles packet chains; descriptor sorter `0x001c81a8` buckets/reorders queued entries by context key. This narrows the VU search to the shared submission path but does not identify the `MSCAL 0` program. The UG2 solid loader stores `0x134018` as 8-byte records and `0x134019` as 12-byte records. Tracing `0x001bf018` → `0x001bed88` → `0x001e1948` → `0x001e1548` shows these are normal-smoothing inputs, not face indices: the engine merges the 8-byte rows by hash, applies the 12-byte rows’ masks, averages and normalizes signed-byte XYZ normals, then writes the packed normal back to a strip vertex in VIF data (only when the strip has flag `0x4000`). In the first Skyline solid, the arrays contain 98 and 126 records with the same 66 distinct hash keys. The strip table declares 455 polygons and polygroup numbers 0–10. UG2 geometry streams end in `MSCAL 0`. Correction to the earlier VU-code lead: static DMA data at UG2 VA 0x0044cc30 (file offset 0x0034dc30) contains eight VIF MPG uploads at data offsets +0x1c, +0x824, +0x102c, +0x1834, +0x203c, +0x2844, +0x304c, and +0x3854. They load sequential blocks at VU addresses 0x000 through 0x700: seven 256-instruction blocks plus a final 204 instructions (1,996 total). Render setup 0x001cfbf0 links this packet, and geometry streams end in MSCAL 0, strongly matching this payload to the mesh program invoked at address 0. The microprogram payload is identified. A temporary standalone VU disassembly shows entry PC 0x0000 branches into initialization at PC 0x0318; the data path reads VIF bounds at VU addresses 2/3 and positions at 5, reads normals at 0x4d, applies integer-to-float conversion and approximately 1/127 scaling, and emits XGKICK output packets. The transform/normalization and packet-output stages are now visible. At PC 0x02e8, the program launches XGKICK streams from VU memory addresses 638 and 735; it contains additional XGKICK paths. The VU path at PC 0x14c8-0x14f8 builds a GIFtag: it forms `0x8000 | vi01` and stores that as the low 32-bit word, then stores the remaining lanes from VU memory at address 615. This sets GIFtag EOP and takes NLOOP from `vi01`, loaded from the current input descriptor at `vi12+0`. A second path at PC 0x3018-0x3058 builds the same low word from `vi11` and writes it to a separate tag. Both paths copy the remaining lanes from VU memory, but the PRIM field and how these dynamic counts correspond to strip metadata still need tracing. The first Skyline strip packet unpacks bounds to 0x02/0x03, positions at 0x05, UVs at 0x1d, colors at 0x2d and normals at 0x4d, then calls MSCAL 0; with the observed TOPS basis it does not directly initialize VU address 615. The tag lanes therefore come from other VU state or a different VIF base/offset setup, still to be traced. The streams relationship to NumPolys remains unresolved. Other branches kick the same fixed buffers and a dynamic `vi13` address; the initial path sets `vi13` to zero. The visible static stores do not directly write the first qword at addresses 638/735, so tracing requires following the active VU control path and indirect output pointer. The GIFtag count construction is now identified, but has not yet been correlated with strip `NumPolys`. Renderer setup 0x001d82f8 separately references PTR_DAT_0044cc30 as a fallback source in a GS image-transfer packet; that use does not negate the VIF MPG uploads in the same static data. Next trace the writers to the XGKICK buffers and decode GIFtag NLOOP/PRIM fields to identify primitive mode/count and correlate these with strip restart rules and NumPolys before implementing the OpenGL renderer  New VIF layout check resolved a missing decoder case: full groups use V4-16 SoA; the last 1, 2, or 3 coordinates are respectively encoded as three S-16 scalars (axis-major), three V2-16 vectors (axis-major), or three V3-16 vectors (vertex-major). The point decoder now handles all three tail forms; offline validation decodes all 174,708 declared SKYLINE vertices. This corrects positions supplied to future topology work, but does not resolve triangle connectivity.  A topology experiment used `(NumVerts - NumPolys)/2` as the number of candidate triangle-strip runs and chose run boundaries at the largest legal adjacent-position gaps. This generated the expected 107,890 table polygons, but both depth-sorted offline output and the live SDL preview remained badly streaked; the heuristic is rejected for the runtime renderer. Static VU dataflow shows the mesh program reads `NumVerts` and the packed VIF group count from the address-0 metadata, then writes `NumVerts` into GIFtag NLOOP; it does not receive the table's `NumPolys` byte. The table therefore constrains a possible decomposition but does not establish run boundaries or primitive mode. |

| 89 | 2026-10-17 | Geometry | NOTE | Plain-strip topology test and the run-count constraint it confirms. Drawing each strip packet as one alternating triangle strip (152,216 triangles overall) yields long streaks spanning the model, so the vertex sequence is not one strip. `(NumVerts - NumPolys)/2` is a positive integer for every one of the 11,246 strips (33,409 runs / 107,890 polygons overall), so each `eStripEntry` is that many triangle-strip runs partitioning its own vertices; the earlier largest-gap heuristic fails because most entries have several similar-magnitude gaps (`tools` gap dump: SKYLINE strip 5 needs 8 runs but shows a single ~0.96 jump; strip 4 needs 2 and shows one clear 0.93 jump). Run boundaries are therefore not recoverable from position gaps alone. Next: trace the mesh VU program's per-run loop / XGKICK writers, or look for a per-run count encoded in the packet. `tools/vif_dump.py` review fixes: `solid_preview.py` imports it but it was untracked (now added); corrected applied UNPACK signedness, `NUM=0` (256 elements), and the unhandled MPG payload skip | offline `/tmp/test-strip.png` (point-only `tools/solid_preview.py` output unchanged); native `build` up to date, `bware_test` passes, direct `UI_QRCarSelect.fng` launch decodes 73,693 SKYLINE vertices and exits 0 |
| 90 | 2026-10-17 | Geometry/Runtime | FIXED (see #110) | Car mesh triangle topology is still unresolved exactly, so a documented functional approximation was adopted and the native strip decoder fixed. Each `eStripEntry` is drawn as one triangle strip of `NumVerts` vertices with the `(NumVerts-2)-NumPolys` largest-longest-edge triangles dropped as the run-seam bridge triangles, yielding exactly `NumPolys` triangles per strip. Evidence: `(NumVerts-NumPolys)/2` is a positive integer for all 11,246 strips; strip `Flags` take only three constant values (`0x4080/0x4180/0xc180`), `PolyGroupNumber` does not track run count, and every VIF strip block ends in its own `MSCAL 0` (one VU invocation per strip); 5,812 of 7,298 multi-run strips separate cleanly by longest edge and 1,486 do not, so seam positions remain a heuristic (the VU receives only `NumVerts`, never `NumPolys`). Implemented offline in `tools/solid_mesh.py` and natively in `FENativeInterface` (`BuildStripTriangles` + `SDL_RenderGeometry`, filled and depth-shaded). Also fixed three native decode bugs that had dropped ~58% of strips: the VIF chunk's 8-byte `0x11` (FLUSHE) prefix was not skipped, so every strip base was 8 bytes early and bounds at VU addresses 0x2/0x3 were missed for 5,468 strips; the tail-vertex read index was off by 5 (`soa/4*3` is an index, not a VU address); and V4-32 bounds (`comps==4`) plus truncated final unpacks are now tolerated. Result: native decodes 107,890/107,890 SKYLINE triangles (was 45,425) and renders a solid car. EE corroboration: `0x001d3f78`/`0x001d43f8` enqueue each whole strip (`mesh VIF base + VifOffset*16`, size `NumVerts`) with no run split, so the VU receives only `NumVerts` (never `NumPolys`) and the exact run rule would need VU simulation (out of scope), leaving seams a heuristic. A cut-based (adjacent-pair) seam selection was tested and is not better (43,902 vs 43,020 long kept edges), so the largest-edge drop stands. The decoder fix generalizes: 10 cars (SKYLINE 107,890; 350Z 109,764; TT 106,715; MIATA 112,546; TIBURON 100,474; FOCUS 102,083; CORSA 104,206; COROLLA 101,617; AUDIO 15,722; COUPE 989) all decode `declared==decoded` triangles with 0 bad strips | `tools/solid_mesh.py`; also see #91 |
| 91 | 2026-10-17 | Geometry/Frontend | FIXED (see #110) | Definitive packet-level proof that strip run boundaries are VU-computed, and the car-select preview switched to depth-sorted splats. A full VIF command trace of one multi-run strip (`mesh0 strip5`, `nv=29 npl=13 R=8`) shows the packet contains only: VU addr 0 metadata `[nv,(nv-1)>>2,7,lmi*4]`, addr 2/3 bounds (V3-32), addr 4 a V4-32 vector, addr 5.. positions as V4-16 SoA groups of 4, addr 29/43 constant `(16,3948,..)` UV-ish data, addr 45 `V4-8 num=nv` normals, addr 77 `V4-8 num=24` more attribute data — there is no index or per-run count array anywhere, yet the decoded 29 positions form a smooth polyline (a single large positional jump), so runs are neither stored nor recoverable from position gaps. `R` is only derivable from the table. Post-hoc bridge filters all fail to clean the mesh (fixed-count largest-edge drop, adjacent-pair `min` drop, triangle-normal/dihedral discontinuity, and `k`x median-edge ratio all leave streaks), because bridges between spatially separate runs vary in length. Exact topology therefore requires executing the mesh VU microprogram (out of project scope). Native `FENativeInterface::RenderCarPreviewOverlay` now draws the decoded vertices as a cached depth-sorted splat buffer (opaque 5.2px quads, nearest-last painter order) instead of triangles; `LoadCarPreview` still decodes and validates the exact `NumPolys` count. Result: the car-select preview is a coherent solid car body (window/roof gap remains) instead of a streaked blob. Offline cross-check (`/tmp/splat-2.png`) and native capture (`/tmp/splat-native2.png`); `bware_test` passes | `src/game/indep/Frontend/FENative.cpp` (`RenderCarPreviewOverlay`); `/tmp/splat-native2.png`; `/tmp/singlestrip.png` (single-strip cluster+2 far vertices); `/tmp/fr2-SKYLINE-cull.png`, `/tmp/quad-quadgrp.png`, `/tmp/ratio-2.5.png` (failed filters) |

| 108 | 2026-10-18 | Render | WORKAROUND | **OpenGL world renderer + world textures.** SDL's render driver is forced to `opengl` with a 24-bit depth buffer; `TrackWorld::RenderGL` (`World/TrackWorldGL.cpp`) flushes SDL's queue, saves the GL state SDL caches (attrib/client-attrib stacks, matrices, `GL_CURRENT_PROGRAM`, array-buffer binding), draws the world with fixed-function GL 1.5 (per-64 m-cell vertex buffers built on first sight, depth test, linear fog 200-900 m, cell cone culling), then restores the state so the FEng HUD keeps drawing through SDL_Renderer; the software painter path remains the fallback (`NFSU2_SOFTWARE_WORLD=1`). 60 fps (vsync) with a 900 m draw distance (was ~45 fps at 450 m). **Textures**: each solid's `0x134012` chunk is a list of `(texture name hash, 0)` pairs indexed by `eStripEntry::TextureNumber`; the stream's 589 TPKs (`0xb3300000`, 1,612 textures) are kept and decoded on first use. VU layout per strip is fixed: positions 0x05-0x1c, **UVs from 0x1d, two vertices per V4-16 qword `(u0, v0, u1, v1)`, 4096 = 1.0** (checked against a 4-vertex SoA alternative: edge metres-per-UV spread 0.28 vs 0.37 log units), **pre-lit vertex colours from 0x2d** (V4-8 unsigned, 128 = 1.0; world alpha byte is 0x41, not used). Texture alpha follows the TPK info (`+0x55` AlphaUsageType, `+0x56` AlphaBlendType, as MW's TextureInfo): usage 1 = punch-through (alpha test; trees, fences, foliage), usage 2 + blend 1 = alpha blend (chain-link), usage 2 + blend 2 = additive (streetlight glows, pulse planes, drawn in a second pass without depth writes), everything else opaque -- road/sidewalk textures are "usage 2 + blend 0" and their alpha (32-180) is not opacity (treating it as opacity showed the sky through the road). Gaps: some facades look smeared (bridge triangles from the topology approximation, #90, stretch UVs across runs); cars are untextured; no texture animation/scroll; mip bias/`TilableUV` not applied | `build/png/tex1.png`; `tests/texture_test` now takes `*` or `#hash` |

| 110 | 2026-10-18 | Geometry | FIXED | **Exact PS2 strip topology -- resolves #65/#90/#91.** Strip restarts are not stored as flags: the mesh VU microprogram (8 MPG blocks at EE 0x0044cc30; disassembly kept in `~/re/nfsu2_pal/vu/meshvu.asm`) seeds the VU **random-number register R** from the four 32-bit words unpacked to VU address 1 (`RINIT R, vf23.x/.z/.w`, VU 0x1560/0x1570/0x15b0/0x15f0) and steps it once per vertex (`RNEXT.w`, an LFSR: `R = ((R<<1) ^ bit4 ^ bit22) & 0x7fffff | 0x3f800000`); the vertex's XYZ2 qword takes R as its W word, so **bit 15 of each R value is the GS ADC (no-kick) bit**. Dispatch at VU 0x1558: `JR` into an unrolled `JALR` list entered at 0x1618 - 0x10*(groups-1); the `JR`'s delay slot seeds from .x and the delay slots of the JALRs at 0x1568/0x15a8/0x15e8 reseed from .x/.z/.w *before* their call; the software-pipelined vertex routines (0x1628 first, then 0x1898/0x1740) perform 7 RNEXTs on the first call (3 of them pipeline prologue) and 4 on each later call. Seed usage matches the data exactly (1-3 groups: x; 4: w; 5-7: x+w; 8: z+w; .y is the world solids' 1/256 m position step). Verified: kicks == `eStripEntry::NumPolys` for **11,246/11,246 SKYLINE and 128,906/128,906 L4RA strips** (`tools/strip_topology.py`). The native `SolidMesh::StripRestartFlags` emits one triangle per kicked vertex (the old longest-edge approximation remains only as a guard, unused on this data). Exact topology also exposed a decode bug: **3-vertex tails are axis-major** (one V3-16 qword per axis, like the 1/2-vertex tails), not vertex-major -- the old reading put 156 of 174 L4RA 3-tail strips' last vertices outside their bounds. Result: cars render as clean solid bodies (car-select sliver filter removed), roads have no holes, facades no longer smear. | 123 | 2026-10-18 | Sound | WORKAROUND | **Music.** The game was silent apart from the movies. `SOUND\PFDATA\MUSICSFX.MUS` (265,576,064 bytes) is **43 concatenated EA SCHl streams**: SCHl header in the "GSTR" variant (`GSTR` + 4 bytes, then tag/length/value records: 0x82 channels, 0x84 sample rate, 0x85 sample count, 0xFD sub-block, 0xFF end -- no codec tag), SCCl (block count), SCDl blocks, SCEl end. SCDl body: u32 BE samples in the block, u32 BE offset per channel, then per channel **EA-XA v2** frames -- `0xEE` + 2 BE history samples + 28 BE PCM samples (61 bytes), or a coefficient/shift byte (coefficient pair index in the high nibble from {0,0}{240,0}{460,-208}{392,-220}, shift = low nibble + 8) + 28 4-bit samples high nibble first (15 bytes). Stream 0: 32 kHz stereo, 147.7 s; frames consume every channel block exactly (200/200 checked), output is smooth (diff/rms 0.12), unclipped, peaks to ~17k. FFmpeg's `ea` demuxer rejects the GSTR header, so the decoder is native: `Sound/MusicStream` reads the file block by block from the archive (the 32-bit process cannot hold it), decodes into an SDL audio queue (~0.75 s ahead) and plays the streams in order, looping; it starts at the first main menu (or a directly entered race), pauses with the race pause menu, and `NFSU2_MUSIC_VOLUME` scales it (0 = off). `tools/ea_mus.py` decodes a stream to WAV for checking. Not done: the interactive music map `MUSICSFX.MPF` (song order, menu vs race music, EA TRAX jukebox titles), engine sounds (`CAR_nn_ENG_MB_EE/SPU.abk`), speech (`SOUND\SPEECH\UG2_SPEECH.BIG/.IDX/.CSI/.EVT`), FE sounds (`FE_SOUND_*` events), FX reverb presets (`SOUND\FXEDIT\*.FX`) | `tools/ea_mus.py` |
| 124 | 2026-10-18 | Sound | WORKAROUND | **Player engine sound.** `SOUND\ENGINE\CAR_00..18_ENG_MB_SPU.abk` / `_EE.abk` (19 banks): EA "ABKC" banks (header +0x18/+0x20 = 0x2d00 bank offset) with a **BNKl** sub-bank -- 'BNKl', u16 version 5, u16 sound count, u32 header size, u32 total size, u32, then per sound a u32 offset (from its slot) to a "PT" header (`PT` + platform 05 + 0, then tag/length/BE value: 0x80 version, 0x84 rate, 0x85 samples, 0x86/0x87 loop, 0x88 data offset from the BNKl, 0xFF end; no codec tag = PS2 SPU ADPCM). CAR_00: 7 loops -- 0 idle (10003 Hz, 1.48 s, ~33 Hz fundamental), 1-3 and 4-6 at ~16 kHz (fundamentals 67/133/67 Hz), read as on-/off-throttle sets at rising engine speed; slot 7 empty. The `_EE.abk` half carries R5900 code (EA's engine synthesis module, not ported). `Sound/EngineSound` decodes the loops (VAG) and mixes them in an SDL callback: idle loop near idle rpm, three triangular rpm bands (centres 15/50/85% of idle..redline) of the on- or off-throttle set (0.15 s blend), each pitched by rpm / the band's rpm, smoothed gains, volume 0.3. Fed from the car drivetrain (#120) every frame; silent while paused or out of a race; muted with `NFSU2_MUSIC_VOLUME=0`. | 129 | 2026-10-18 | Physics | FIXED | **Real vehicle physics: ported and racing** (replaces the arcade stand-in of #105/#120 for the player car when `NFSU2_PHYSICS=ug2`). The per-frame update is `0x00317428` (vtable 0x0047ff88): fixed substeps of an explicit-Euler integrator (`0x0030a688`) over integrable objects -- RigidBody, 4 Suspensions, 4 Tyres, Engine, Drivetrain -- built by the vehicle ctor `0x00316900` from the car record (parameter block = record + 0x118: body rec+0x228, tyres rec+0x1e8/+0x208, suspension rec+0x288/+0x2a8, drivetrain rec+0x2c8, engine rec+0x308, aero rec+0x378). The derivative tick `0x00318208` solves tyre forces in 5 iterations (`0x003183a0`) and clamps them with a friction circle (`0x00324460`, surface table 0x0052f954). Suspension (`0x00324ff8`) decoded: progressive spring + bump/rebound damping + anti-roll between axle pairs. The call tree is 251 functions / ~56 KB (pseudocode all present). Map and offsets in `docs/physics.md`; class names from strings RigidBody / Suspension / TwoWheelAckermanSteering. Ghidra helpers `CallTree.java`, `Xrefs.java`. **Implemented** (`src/game/indep/Physics/UG2Vehicle.cpp`, ~640 lines): every parameter from the record; RigidBody with the game's Euler substeps (0.0044 s, capped at 40); 4 probe suspensions (progressive spring, bump/rebound, anti-roll, slope load) on the terrain via `TrackWorld::GroundHeight`; slip-curve tyres with a friction circle; engine/clutch/gearbox (modes 0-2 disengage/select/engage, auto shift up at the shift point / down below the down point); static-initializer curve tables replayed with `tools/mips_init_run.py`. Three bugs found and fixed: (1) the clutch lurch (an upshift used to drive the wheels hard backwards through the gearbox, now modes 0-2 transmit `te*G*clutch` and free-rev the engine); (2) the wheel-spin correction was skipped whenever the tyre was in the *lateral* sliding branch, so in every hard corner the driven wheels drifted to a lock and the sliding friction braked the car -- replaced by a per-step friction-budget longitudinal model (the wheel is pinned to the ground speed unless the demanded force exceeds the grip left after the lateral force, then it spins / locks); (3) no world collision, so the car drove straight through barriers and off the track into the void -- added a swept wall test (`TrackWorld::HitsWall` now returns the surface normal) that cancels the velocity into the wall so the car scrapes along barriers. Stand-ins (marked in code): the 5-pass solve `0x003183a0`, the steering input filter, body/world collision `0x334110`, floor recovery off the loaded terrain. Verified: autodrive on L4RA 4012 (`NFSU2_PHYSICS=ug2 NFSU2_AUTODRIVE=1`) finished the 2-lap race **1st of 4 in 2:30.36 (best lap 1:13.38)** at 60 fps (`NFSU2_WORLD_DEBUG=1` fps). Not yet ported: the 5-pass solve, real collision geometry, player steering filter, drift assist 0x00317330, nitrous/turbo, surface types, engine rock | `src/game/indep/Physics/UG2Vehicle.{h,cpp}`, `docs/physics.md` |

New modules: `Career/Memcard` is the port's memory card - a card is a directory, slot 1 is `<NFSU2_DATA>/mc0` (`NFSU2_MEMCARD` overrides it), a save is a `<NAME>.SAV` file, capacity 8 files (stand-in). It replaces the console stack UG2 goes through: `FEPkgMemcard` create `0x00225f50` / ctor `0x00226720` / dispatch `0x00228700`, `FEPkgMemcardList` `0x00228348`, `MemcardAllocator`, `MemcardThread`, `MMSaveLoad`, `MemcardBootScreen`. `Career/Profile` is the career profile - what UG2 keeps at `0x004f1744`, `0xac54` bytes per player (`&DAT_004f1744 + player * 0xac54` in `0x001e8d00`/`0x001e9ba8`/`0x0025ece0`/`0x001f4b90`/...; the item pool is reached through `0x00275a28`/`0x002759a0`, the save subsystem through the `0x004e8710` manager) - with name, money, respect, stage, region, owned cars (model + paint), current car and play time. **The on-card byte layout is EA's serialized object graph and is not decoded** (the disc ships no sample console save), so the file format is the port's own (magic `U2CAREER`, version, size, checksum). Flow: `DAT_004f0e70` (career present) now comes from the card, so Resume Career appears after a restart; New Career (`0x00259328`, mode `0x21961`) and Career Garage (`0x002592c0`, mode `0xc11`) route through the real `MC_Main.fng` package, where the port performs the save/load natively and continues on Accept (Cancel returns to `UI_StartCareer.fng`); New Career writes the profile at once and, on the career car-select Accept, records the starter car and saves again; Resume (`0x00259300`) loads the profile, restores the saved car into the car preview (`FENativeInterface::SetPreviewCar`) and enters the city. Not ported: the `MC_Main`/`MC_List` UI itself (its class builds the file list and the card controls from the memcard library, `0x00228990`/`0x00226908`), the profile name entry, car prices (not in any decoded chunk, so the starter car is free), and career events/shops/respect changes (`0x34a11`, #136). Verified: New Career -> `MC_Main.fng` -> `PLAYER.SAV` (56 B) -> car select -> starter car SKYLINE (`68` B) -> city; restart -> 3 options -> Resume loads "money 1000, respect 200, 1 cars" -> city with the saved car. `bware_test` passes | `src/game/indep/Career/Memcard.{h,cpp}`, `Profile.{h,cpp}`, `FEStartup.{h,cpp}`; captures `/tmp/opencode/career_city2.png` (city), `/tmp/opencode/mc_step.png` (the `MC_Main.fng` step), `/tmp/opencode/resume2.png` (Resume); test aid `NFSU2_MEMCARD` |

| 139 | 2026-10-19 | Frontend/Career | WORKAROUND | **MC_Main.fng / MC_List.fng (the memory-card screens) ported.** `MC_Main.fng` (create `0x00225f50`, ctor `0x00226720`, dispatch `0x00228700`) is the card status dialog the career options open (#137): its status string `0x1e2640fa` now shows the native card (`"Memory card (PS2) in MEMORY CARD slot 1: N save(s)"`), the dispatch's tick branch relabels `0x47ff4e7c` with the `"CALL"` text (`0x566640` -> `0x00293378`/`0x00293240`) and polls the card every 0x15 ticks (`0x0023d570`/`0x0023d9b0`, here read once on load, after the New Career profile is written). Accept opens the browser (`0x00225fb8` pushes `MC_List.fng`). `MC_List.fng` (create `0x002286b8`, ctor `0x00228348`) is the file list: 8 rows, each a group (`0x66e29936`..`3d`: backing `0xa67c3fe2` + two 36x36 icons) with a name string on the left (`0xb2980ba8`..`af`, x -7) and a value string on the right (`0x9b483b01`..`08`, x 415). EA's class creates a widget per card entry (`0x00228990`, filtered by `0x00226908`) from three per-row name templates (`0x466e60`/`0x467160`/`0x465ac8`), so each row's objects hash consecutively 1..8; the port has no widget system and drives those package objects directly (Up/Down moves the cursor by row alpha 255/170, Accept uses the highlighted save, Back closes the list). Three engine behaviours had to be handled: (1) `FEngine::GetFirstPackage()` is the **bottom** of the stack - `FEPackageList::AddPackage` sorts ascending by priority and `PushPackage` gives a pushed screen priority+1 - so the card screens ask for the top package, not the first; (2) the dialog ships at alpha 0 with the authoring tool's placeholder colours (a group with `Col.a == 0` makes `FEngine::RenderGroup` skip the whole subtree) and EA's class fades it in; the port reveals the frame/rows and **re-applies the reveal in `FEStartupSequence::PreRender` just before `FEngine::Render`**, because the package's own script tracks rewrite the colours/alphas every frame (without it the whole dialog draws with a red cast and hidden rows reappear); (3) `QueuePackageSwitch` (command 3 in `FEngine::ProcessPackageCommands`) only unloads the package that currently has control, and after the push `MC_Main` has mask 0, so the card screens unload both explicitly (`CloseMemcardScreens`) or `MC_Main` stays drawn under the next screen. Verified: New Career -> `MC_Main.fng` ("1 save(s)", CALL) -> Accept -> `MC_List.fng` ("PLAYER" row over the dimmed dialog) -> Accept -> `UI_QRCarSelect.fng`; Up/Down moves the highlight; Back closes the list, and Back on `MC_Main` returns to `UI_StartCareer.fng` (career state survives). Not done: the right-hand row column sits outside the 640x480 frame in this layout (left alone), the visible buttons keep the package's `Yes/No` language defaults (EA's class sets them per card operation at runtime; only the off-screen `0x47ff4e7c` gets its `CALL` text), no format/overwrite/name-entry prompts, `MC_Bootup`/`MC_Background` still unused. Dev aid: `NFSU2_FETREE` now also prints each object's name, an `FE_String` object's label hash and its colour | `src/game/indep/Frontend/FEStartup.{h,cpp}`, `Career/Profile.{h,cpp}`, `src/main.cpp`; captures `/tmp/opencode/mc_main_direct.png`, `/tmp/opencode/mclistA.png`, `/tmp/opencode/mc_overlay2.png` |

The table's race numbers are exactly the race database's numbers (`0x34201` `+0x8a`; e.g. 4602 = L4RF "Street X" 489 m), so a finished race finds its event. | 145 | 2026-10-19 | World/Physics | FIXED | **Ground query ported from the game (`0x00336180`): fixes the car riding onto a tunnel roof and stalling (race 4201).** Root cause: `GroundHeight` ranked render triangles (no reliable facing -- strip winding alternates and the PS2 does not cull) with a loose collision-mesh decode, so a tunnel roof/bridge underside within the suspension probe counted as ground, lifted the car, and a portal wall stopped it mid-air. Decoded from `0x00336180/0x00335328/0x00335098`: each collision section (`0x80034130`) has a header (`0x34131`: u16 cols@+4, rows@+6, f32 origin x,y @+8/+0xc), 32 m cells (`0x34137`: {u16 first, u16 count}) into an index list (`0x34136`: {u16 face, u16 mask}, mask = bit `(1<<(x/8 mod 32)) << ((y/8 mod 8)*4)` of the 8 m sub-cell) over the 32-byte faces (`0x34134`; verified for all 100 L4RG sections, no out-of-range entry). Rules: skip faces whose group byte (+0) is non-zero (race barrier groups; the enable table `DAT_00523ddc` is not ported, so they stay off) and flag bits `0x0a` (the 10,688 vertical wall faces, `flags 0x12/0x16`; flag `0x04` makes a face unbounded in Z); a point is inside when the max of the edge functions is <= 0 (`0x00335328`) -- only counter-clockwise (upward) faces pass, which excludes ceilings; height = `(n.v0 - nx x - ny y)/nz`, highest one at or below the query height. All 70,944 L4RG faces wind upward or are vertical walls. `TrackWorld::CollisionGround` implements it; render triangles are only a fallback where the collision mesh has no face. This also removes the old centroid-cell hashing that dropped large faces from neighbouring cells (the 15% "coverage misses" and the missing tunnel-ramp floor of #133 -- the "1 m below the road" reading was the stacked layers). Two vehicle fixes found on the way (UG2Vehicle): the settling drag (`b.rest`) held a car stopped in mid air (~1 m/s fall), and the floor-recovery pinned it at its last ground height even with terrain below. Verified: race 4201 autodrive now runs the tunnel (z 2 -> -10 -> -3) to 955 m at 40 m/s (stalled at 184 m before) and stops at a different obstacle (a 1 m wall at 220,606 -- autodrive corner, not ground); 4012 on L4RA drives normally; `bware_test` passes. Not done: wall collision from the 0x12/0x16 faces (`0x00337888`), the group-enable table, the exact first-hit-within-4 m rule | `TrackWorld.{h,cpp}`, `Physics/UG2Vehicle.{h,cpp}` |
| 146 | 2026-10-19 | World/Physics | FIXED | **Wall collision from the collision mesh (`0x00337888`).** The game's broad phase collects the faces overlapping the move's box from the 32 m cells (8 m sub-cell masks) into three lists by flag: floors (none of `0x0a`), walls (`0x02`) and a third class (`0x08`); the 10,688 L4RG wall faces (`flags 0x12/0x16`, surfaces 1/20) are the walls, bit `0x04` making them unbounded in Z (the decoder sets Z to -2048/+2048). `TrackWorld::CollisionWall` tests the segment against them (two triangles per quad, normal facing the start) and `HitsWall` now uses those plus the race barriers a race enabled (`Tri::barrier`); the scenery's own steep render triangles (kerbs, poles, tunnel linings) no longer count as walls (#133 had rejected the collision mesh for walls because it also fed ground; the wall subset is the game's own). Barrier groups (face byte +0 != 0) stay off. Verified: 4012 autodrive unchanged (race 1348 m at 60 s, same as before), driving left off the grid stops against a wall (0.3 m/s at 698,-551), 4201 still passes the tunnel (the remaining 4201 stop at 222,605 is a real wall hit at 40 m/s: autodrive does not slow for that corner); `bware_test` passes | `TrackWorld.{h,cpp}` |

| 157 | 2026-10-19 | Frontend/Career | FIXED | **Quick Race track unlock rule (applied).** `0x00271a90(flags, a, b)` (the unlock query behind the QR track list; `iGpffffdf68 != 0` unlocks everything): flag bit 0 -> `0x00271018` -> `0x0026b438(profile, race)`; bits 1-2 -> always true (`0x002714d0`); bits 3-8 -> `0x00271138` -> `0x00271018` again. `0x0026b438` walks every 0x34a11 event whose race list holds the race (`0x002635d8`) and is true if **any** of them passes `0x0026b3a8(event, race)`: the race's 4-byte slot (`event + 0x18 + i*4`, `{s16 race, u8 flags, u8}`) has **flag bit 1 (`0x00263858`, value 2) set -> unlocked from the start** (bit 0 is the reverse direction; 9 of the 224*3 slots, e.g. `(2,x)`), otherwise the event is complete (`0x00268bd8` on the event id). So a track is playable in Quick Race when it is a starter track or its career event has been completed. Not applied in the port: the QR list shows every track (the original's lock presentation -- hidden vs. lock icon -- is not read yet), and without a career profile the rule would leave only the starter tracks. A `QuickRace` filter would be `Career::EventTable::TrackUnlocked(race, completedEvents)` = any event with that race has slot flag bit 1 or is complete | `Career/Events.{h,cpp}`, `Frontend/FEStartup.{h,cpp}`, `main.cpp` | **Resolution (2026-10-19):** applied. The lock presentation is the padlock icon, not a hidden row: `0x001facc0` stores `(0x00271a90(...) == 0)` at the list item's `+8`, the icon selector `0x001f9d28` returns `0xf05704ac` for those items (the category icons are unchanged), and the Accept path `0x001fa6e8` returns early when the highlighted item's `+8` is set. `EventTable::TrackUnlocked` implements `0x0026b438`/`0x0026b3a8` over the table (the slot flag byte at record `+0x1a`: bit 0 reverse `0x00263848`, bit 1 starter `0x00263858`); the QR list keeps all offered tracks, draws the padlock for locked ones and refuses Accept. The card profile is now read at boot (`FEStartupSequence::LoadBootProfile`, after the event table so the dev aids see it), mirroring the console, so career progress feeds the rule. | 177 | 2026-10-20 | World/Race | FIXED | **Race routes now follow the segment link graph instead of every road in the Paths file.** `0x34149` segments carry two endpoint link lists (`+0x22` counts; `+0x24`/`+0x44` 8-byte entries, u16 segment index + pointer written by `0x00345418`), and a `PATHS<n>.BIN` holds both carriageways and neighbouring events' roads: 4001's 40 segments chain into 19,898 m open (three laps of the same tarmac), 4088 into 12,913 m, 4041 into 11,876 m. The old `+0x14` order field is a per-file segment id, not route order. `RaceRoute::Load` now builds the link graph and depth-first searches simple cycles/open paths, scoring by the race record's `trackLength` (`±max(40 m, 5%)`) with a 3 m penalty per segment traversed against its stored point order (lane hops across the central reservation). All 60 L4RA races were swept: every route is within ~1% of its record (4001 6275/6274 closed, 4002 5839/5837, 4012 1872/1869, 4088 7342/7344 open, 4041 5058/5008, 4126 9962/9960); no order fallback. This also resolves #166's reverse cap: 4001 reverse is now the same 6275 m closed loop, autodrive passes the old 491 m point (516 m with `NFSU2_AI_LANE=3`) and reverse 4311 finishes 1st in 0:51.96 (laps 0:31.90 / 0:20.08). `NFSU2_AI_LANE=<m>` offsets the autodrive player's line (the centre line clips an always-on group-0 bollard at ~(22,300) on 4001 reverse); `NFSU2_ROUTE_DEBUG=1` dumps the chosen route points. A new synthetic `route_test` has a direct 400 m loop and a 441 m detour sharing endpoints, checks the selected branch and the reversed lap wrap; `ctest` 5/5 and the disc self-test pass | `World/RaceRoute.{h,cpp}`, `main.cpp`, `tests/route_test.cpp`, `CMakeLists.txt` |

| 187 | 2026-10-20 | Frontend/Shop | WORKAROUND | **The Body Shop keeps a career item pool: a bought part stays owned, and refitting it on any car is free.** The original tests ownership with `0x002b5b00` before selling a part; #185/#186 stood that in with "the same part is already fitted", so switching away and back recharged the player and lost the previous part. The port now stores a flat pool in the profile (`Profile::ownedParts`), one `uint32_t` key per part: bits 0..7 shop part id (`0xc` spoiler, `7` roof scoop, `0x10` exhaust tip), 8..15 style, bit 24 carbon, bit 25 roof OFFSET, bit 26 roof DUAL, matching `CarPart`. Accept is now: the part on the car is a no-op; an owned part is installed for free; an unowned part checks the balance, deducts the price and adds the key. Only a successful install saves, and the preview is refreshed either way. `RememberFittedParts` seeds the pool from the car's three fitted parts when the shop opens and again before a replacement, so a v1-v8 save (or a part fitted before this change) does not lose its wing/scoop/tip or get charged for it a second time. Profile v9 appends `{count, keys...}` after the v8 block (v1-v8 saves load with an empty pool; a corrupt count above 4096 is rejected). This also fixes the no-career-car path: the shop no longer deducts money when `ShopCar()` is null, because the old guard only looked at `m_profile.valid`. Verified on copies of the career save with `NFSU2_EXTRA_CARS=SKYLINE`: buying `JETSTREAM L1` logs `fitted roof scoop style 1 (JETSTREAM L1, $100), career $900` and writes a 232-byte v9 save; buying `HEADWIND L1` logs `$100`, `career $800` and a 236-byte save; going back to `JETSTREAM` logs `fitted roof scoop style 1 (JETSTREAM L1, owned), career $800` and does not charge; a further Accept logs `JETSTREAM L1 is already fitted`; relaunching loads money 800 with the pool intact, shows `HEADWIND` on the preview, and Accept on `HEADWIND` logs `(HEADWIND L1, owned), career $800`. Captures `/tmp/opencode/bs187_owned.png`, `bs187_reload.png`, `bs187_flow.png` with `.log` files. `profile_test` checks the v9 key round-trip and the v5 empty-pool fallback; `ctest` 6/6 and the disc self-test pass. Not done: the pool only covers the three mounted body parts (spoilers, roof scoops, exhaust tips); the shop does not mark owned items or offer an inventory screen; other slot cards, carbon materials, the L4 $0 styles and the insufficient-funds dialog remain as noted in #185/#186 | `Frontend/FEStartup.{h,cpp}`, `Career/Profile.{h,cpp}`, `tests/profile_test.cpp` |

Relaunching restores all three on the preview (`SKYLINE_STYLE02_HOOD_A`, `_STYLE01_HEADLIGHT_L/R`, `_STYLE01_BRAKELIGHT_`), opens each card with its fitted item selected (`selected 0`), and Accept on each logs `... is already fitted`; a world free-run with `NFSU2_PART_HOOD=2 NFSU2_PART_HEADLIGHT=3 NFSU2_PART_TAILLIGHT=4` logs the same three panels in `PlayerCar`, so the race path uses the preview selection. Captures `/tmp/opencode/bs188_hood.png`, `bs188_hl.png`, `bs188_tl.png`, `bs188_buy.png`, `bs188_reload2.png`, `bs188_owned.png`, `bs188_split.png`, `bs188_carbhood.png` with `.log` files. `partcatalog_test` gains a per-car pack whose model table overrides the pack order (styles 9/2/7) and `profile_test` the v10 round-trip plus the v5 stock-panel fallback; `ctest` 6/6 and the disc self-test pass. Accept logs `fitted wide body kit style 1 (VAPOR L4, $0), career $499300` and writes a 284-byte v12 save; relaunching loads money 499300, opens the card on item 0 and a further Accept logs `VAPOR L4 is already fitted` without charging. Captures `/tmp/opencode/cmp_stock.png` / `cmp_wide.png` (yaw 90), `v_180_stock.png` / `v_180_wide.png`, `p3_buy.png`, `p5_world.png`, `p14_kits.bmp`, plus the `.log` files. `partcatalog_test` gains a `_KITW03` model entry (wide-body style 3) and `profile_test` the v12 round-trip plus the v5 zero fallback; `ctest` 6/6 and the disc self-test pass. Not done: every kit is $0 because the price table has no level-4 tier; the kit's door skins do not restore shut lines and its arches render as flat paint; the kit's own front fascia covers a fitted bumper (a career save with KIT01 front bumper fitted and the kit on gives a front view within 1% of the same car without the bumper, `/tmp/opencode/p1_bumper.png` vs `p2_wide_bumper.png`), so the original's item-mount path (`0x002b2518` / `0x002b3488`) is still needed to know whether a kit suppresses the bumper slot; other cars' KITW sets and the Carbon Fiber card are untested for this slot; item thumbnails are still the package placeholders; and opening the browser directly with `NFSU2_PACKAGE` (no car select) lists the first card unfiltered (30 front bumpers) until the first card change because the preview car is not loaded yet. | `Frontend/PartCatalog.cpp`, `Frontend/FEStartup.cpp`, `Frontend/FENative.{h,cpp}`, `World/PlayerCar.{h,cpp}`, `World/SolidMesh.{h,cpp}`, `Career/Profile.{h,cpp}`, `tests/partcatalog_test.cpp`, `tests/profile_test.cpp`, `tests/solid_test.cpp` |

Lookups mirror `0x00264388` (category, level) and `0x00264500` (package price = sum of part prices). | 200 | 2026-10-22 | Car/Physics | WORKAROUND | **Performance parts now change the car: the nine tuning modifiers of `0x0031b520` are ported (`Frontend/PerfModel.{h,cpp}`).** Findings: the code's "record" base is the file record + 8, so the engine/chassis frame `0x2b0` bytes it copies starts at file offset `0x118` (mass at frame `0x110` = `+0x228`, torque curve at `0x200` = `+0x318`, rpm limits `0x1f0..0x1f8` = `+0x308..+0x310`). The *stock record itself holds the upgrade data* further down (gear-set blocks at frame `+0x310 + 0x40*level`, curve deltas `+0x420/+0x4e0/+0x510`, suspension `+0x5c0/+0x5e0`, wheel blends `+0x540/+0x560`, handling packs `+0x590/+0x5a0`), so no separate part database is needed. `0x0031a9e0` gives each category's strength: the sum of the installed parts' 0x34a15 stat floats per stat index (levels 1..3, `-1` = unused), clamped to 0..1 when the mean leaves that range. Modifiers in order: cat 0 weight (`0x0031baa0`: mass x ((f-1)*s+1)), 1 transmission (`bb50`: gear block chosen by round(stat2*3), drive ratios), 2 nitrous (`bd28`), 3 curve (`bdb8`), 4 curve (`be80`), 5 chassis (`bf18`), 6 tyres (`c138`), 7 engine (`c1e0`: rpm limits by level + curve), 8 per-wheel (`c358`), 9 handling packs (`c478`). Not ported: `0x0031b720`'s single-part flags (state `+0x28..+0x48`) and the inertia refresh `0x0031e510`. **Behaviour change:** `0x0031be80` runs for every car whether or not a part is fitted, adding the record's stock extra curve (frame `+0x224`) to the torque curve, so the port now builds every car's stock record through `PerfModel::Apply` (`CarRecord::stock`, used for `Performance` and the UG2 physics; `NFSU2_PERF_MODEL=off` restores the stored figures): Skyline 243 -> 299 Nm / 0-100 6.9 -> 5.6 s / 228 -> 249 km/h, 350Z and Civic unchanged. With every level-3 package fitted: Skyline 299 -> 409 Nm / 5.0 s / 293 km/h, Civic 6 gears 296 km/h, Hummer 5 gears 283 km/h (`NFSU2_CAR_TEST=1 NFSU2_CAR_TEST_LEVEL=<n> [NFSU2_CAR_TEST_CAT=<c>]`). The career car's fitted parts (`OwnedCar::perfParts`) tune the player's race car (`[PerfShop] N parts fitted: 0-100 ... top speed ...` at race start); Quick Race cars are stock; opponents/traffic use the stock record. `tests/perfmodel_test.cpp` covers strength, the unconditional stock curve, weight reduction and the curve delta; the disc self-test (`./build/nfsu2 --test`, needs NFSU2_DATA) also pins the real-data figures: 30 packages, stock Skyline 299 Nm, every level-3 package 409 Nm, parts lighten the car. Regression race (NFSU2_AUTODRIVE, 4012 L4RA, 2 laps): model on 4th of 4 in 2:23.18 (best lap 1:09.33), model off (`NFSU2_PERF_MODEL=off`) 4th of 4 in 2:29.24 (1:12.31) - so the old 3rd-in-1:36 baseline of #120 no longer reproduces with or without this change (most likely the route work of #177, 2026-10-20: 4012's route is now the 1869 m record length, and a speed profile of the model-off race shows a clean launch (0-23 m/s in 80 m), ~35-39 m/s on the straights and a braking dip to 12-22 m/s roughly every 250 m, i.e. the autodrive slows for every corner; the #120 baseline predates #177 and is obsolete, not bisected). Not verified against the original numerically (no reference run); the stat bars / Torque-Power preview still use the old stock estimate. |

| 207 | 2026-10-22 | Frontend/Shop | FIXED | **Tuning slider definitions decoded (GLOBALB `0x80034a10` subchunk `0x34a1a`, parser `0x0031f250`): 62 entries of 24 bytes = which installed part unlocks which tuning field and over what range.** Entry: `+0` part id (the same global ids as the 0x34a15 packages, #197), `+4` key `{u8 level, u8 field index, u8 mode, u8 0}`, `+8` / `+0xc` float min / max of the physical modifier, `+0x10` / `+0x14` the slider's UI range (`0.0 .. 10.0` for mode-2 sliders, `-1 .. 1` for mode-4 mixes, `-99.99` = no slider: the modifier is applied by part strength alone). `0x0031f2a0(field, installedParts)` returns the matching entry of the highest installed level. Content: field 0/1/4/5 (+2/6 for the second part of each level, +3/7 as fixed values) with ranges -0.2..0.1 (L1) / -0.3..0.1 (L2) / -0.4..0.2 (L3) belong to the chassis packages (category 5, parts 29-37) = the Suspension sliders; field 8..15 / 23 / 24 are fixed per-part modifiers (categories 1, 3, 4, 7: e.g. part 8 field 9 -0.05..0.10); fields 16/17 (parts 12-14 = category 2, nitrous) are 1..3 / 1..7 / 1..11 (shot count) and 0.4..0.6 / 0.25..0.75 / 0.1..0.9 (mix); field 18 (parts 40/41/44 = category 6) is the -1..1 brake-balance-like mix at 0.4..0.6 / 0.325..0.675 / 0.25..0.75; fields 19/20 (parts 53-55 = category 8, 0..10 sliders with a 0..0 physical range = tuned elsewhere) and 21/22 (parts 56-58 / 65-67 = category 9, the zero-price packs, 0..10 sliders, -0.15..0 / -0.15..0.1) match Tires & Brakes and Aerodynamics. The range engine is `0x0031fa50..0x00322xxx` (`0x003201f0`: per-mode min/max around the current setting, modes 0 fixed step, 1 offset, 2 scaled, 3 mixed, 4 absolute; `0x00320030`: car-derived ends when the table holds -99.99; `0x00321c88/0x00321cb8` clamps) and the per-car settings are 5 slots of 0x29 bytes (`0x002c1338` reads them from the profile car with `0x002775f8`; `0x002c18d8` / `0x002c1960` stash and restore one; `0x002c19e8` = "changed since the stored copy"), so the screens need that engine plus a save field per car. | 208 | 2026-10-22 | Texture/Render | FIXED | **Tiny 4-bit front-end textures were decoded with the wrong layout, which garbled every panel corner, scroll knob and 16x16 icon.** `TexturePack::Decode` treated all 4-bit data as the block-swizzled layout found for the big font glyphs (#11), but textures 16 pixels wide or less (the 16x16 `U2_MINI_ICON` checkmark, the panel/dialog corner pieces, scroll knobs, arrow caps) are stored linearly, one nibble per pixel row by row (a 16x16 texture is exactly 128 bytes): decoding it linearly gives the clean rounded-square / circle shapes, while the 32x32 glyphs (`PS2_CIRCLE`, the button icons) are swizzled and stay correct (a first attempt at `<= 32` broke those; the cut-off is `<= 16`). All front-end screens lose the black speckles at panel corners and the garbled `$` / knob marks (Options, Main Menu, car select, shop screens compared before / after). `NFSU2_TEX_SWIZZLE_ALL=1` restores the old decode; `NFSU2_TEX_DEBUG=<hex hash>` prints a texture's size / bit depth and writes `<NAME>.ppm` (alpha composited over black) in the current directory. The in-game HUD (race 4012: position / laps / time panel, minimap, speedo) was checked after the change and shows no artefacts. Not checked: 8-bit tiny textures (none seen garbled). |
| 209 | 2026-10-22 | Render | NOTE | **The smeared look of world textures in races is not a texture-layout problem.** After #208, `NFSU2_TEX_SCORE=1` (dev aid: world load decodes every texture twice, swizzled and row by row, and counts per `(bits, width, height)` class how many are at least 20% smoother when read linearly - natural images are smooth, a wrong layout is not) was run on L4RA (589 packs, 1611 distinct textures, ~9,000 decodes counting duplicates across packs): **zero** textures in every 4-bit and 8-bit class from 8x16 to 256x256 are smoother linearly, so the block-swizzled decode is right for all sizes above 16 wide (and #208's linear rule is right for the tiny ones). The diagonal streaks and mixed-up patterns on building walls and road surfaces in the 4012 HUD screenshot therefore come from the geometry side (UV coordinates / strip winding of some solids, scenery texture selection or filtering), not from the pixel data. `SetForceLinearDecode` (Texture) is the hook the scorer uses. **Follow-up (2026-10-22):** the spiky object in the 4012 screenshot is the Statue of Liberty's crown (seven designed rays) and its streaky texturing is the low-resolution PS2 mapping on a coarse model, not a decode fault: a spike scorer over all 4225 L4RA solids (`NFSU2_SOLID_SCORE=1`: share of triangles whose longest edge exceeds 60% of the solid's bounding diagonal) flags only 76 solids above 10% (5,553 of 550,897 triangles), and dumping the worst large candidates (`NFSU2_SOLID_DUMP=<hex hash>[,...]` writes `<hash>.obj`; `NFSU2_INST_NEAR=x,y,r` lists placed instances near a point) showed clean buildings / a bridge / a tower. No geometry bug found; the smearing stays a known low-fidelity limit. |
| 210 | 2026-10-22 | Career/Shop | NOTE | **Tuning field -> physics map and apply pass (continues #207).** The 25 tuning fields (table field index of #207) map to the tuned working copy (PerfModel frame of #200) through the accessors `0x00321428` dispatches to (jump table `0x0047f6b0`): field 0 `+0x174`, 1 `+0x178` (x2), 2 `+0x180`, 3 wheel records `+0x18` (stride `0x30`, 4 wheels), 4 `+0x194`, 5 `+0x198` (x2), 6 `+0x1a0`, 7 wheel records `+0x78` (stride `0x30`), 8 `+0x270` (the grip value), 9 `+0x1b8` (x2), 10..15 `+0x1b0 + 4n` (the final-drive / gear block), 16 `+0x2a0` and 17 `+0x2ac` (nitrous), 18 `+0x27c`, 19 `+0xdc` / `+0xe0` and 20 `+0xfc` / `+0x100` (per-axle tyre values), 21 `+0x264` and 22 `+0x268` (the category-9 pair), 23 `+0x200 + 4n` and 24 `+0x224 + 4n` (the two torque-curve arrays). So the Suspension sliders (fields 0-6) drive springs / dampers / ride height, Drivetrain 7-15 the gearing, Nitrous 16/17, Tires & Brakes 18-20, Aerodynamics 21/22. `0x0031fbe0` is the whole apply pass: for fields 0..0x18 it reads each slider's physical value (`0x00320e20`), stores it into the tuning object `+0x40` (`0x0031f400`), re-applies the dependent fields (`0x0031f8d8` / `0x003211f8` / `0x00321548`), re-sums the curve and refreshes (`0x00321850`, `0x00321f98`); `0x0031faf8` says whether a field is active (a table entry exists for an installed part; the gear fields 10..15 only while the gear count allows). Slider position -> value is `base + position * (max - min)` (`0x0031f828`) with `[min, max]` from `0x003201f0` per table mode and `0x00320670` for the car-derived ends. Still to decode before a port: `0x00320670` (1044 bytes, the range source), `0x00320500` (the base value), `0x00321ef0`, `0x00321850`, `0x00321548`, `0x003211f8`, and the five 0x29-byte setting slots in the profile car (`0x002c1338`). |
| 211 | 2026-10-22 | Career/Shop | NOTE | **Tuning save encoding and value mapping (continues #207 / #210).** A setting slot is 41 bytes (`0x29`): one byte per slider, **100 = neutral, 0..200 = position -1..+1** (`0x0031f5e0` reads `(byte - 100) * 0.01` clamped to +-1; `0x0031f658` writes `clamp(int(pos * 100 + 100.5), 0, 200)`): bytes `0..22` = fields 0..22, bytes `0x17..0x1f` = field 23 (9 values), bytes `0x20..0x28` = field 24 (9 values). `0x0031f828` gives a field's value as `ref + pos * (hi - lo)` where `ref` is the tuning object's stored value at `+0x40` (`0x0031f3b0`) and `[lo, hi]` comes from `0x003201f0` (table modes 0 fixed step, 1 offset, 2 scaled, 3 mixed, 4 absolute, clamped to the car-derived ends of `0x00320670`); `0x0031fe88` then maps the user-facing display value linearly onto the physical range (`phys = physLo + (clamp(x) - dispLo) / (dispHi - dispLo) * (physHi - physLo)`), with unit conversions in `0x00321cb8` for the gear / ratio / curve fields (metric vs imperial, gp-relative float constants `fGpffffa204..a224` not read yet) and a sign flip for fields 3, 7, 9 (`0x00321ef0` returns -1). `0x00320500` picks the reference as the min or max over the field's paired fields (0/4, 2/6, 3/7, 0x15/0x16 are mirrored axle pairs). The 0..10 sliders of the table (fields 0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 19-22) are plain 0..10 UI scales; the others show real units. A port therefore needs: the gp-relative constants, `0x00320670` and the profile slot storage, then the list screen; stopped here because inventing the unit scalings would give plausible but unfaithful sliders. |
| 212 | 2026-10-22 | Career | NOTE | **Race-start payment and the car rating (decoded, not ported).** `0x0026cd30(profile, race)` runs when a race is accepted (`0x001fcce8`, `0x001fcdd0`, `0x001fe730` call it right before `0x0026f0d8` / `0x00267e08`) and calls `0x00267ee8(profile, race)`, which pays `int(rating * areaFactor + DAT_00562ed4) + race[+0x26] (s16)` into the bank (`0x0026bbb0(profile + 0x7154, ...)`) and remembers the race's `+0x28` in `DAT_00567cec`. `areaFactor` = the s16 at `+4` of the current area's 0x34a18 record (`0x00269058` via `0x00268ef8`: `profile + area * 0x104 + 0x72fc`, record pointer at `+0x100`) - the "3-star" fields noted in #165. `rating` = `0x00267e48` -> `0x002c8fc8(car)`: the car's total part value (sum of `0x0026b110` over every installed part slot 0..0xa9, with the wide-body / hood-style slots 1, 2, 0xb folded) mapped through a bracket table at `0x00516538` (pairs of {value, rating} with `DAT_00516580` / `DAT_00516584` as the bounds) to a 0..1 fraction, cached in `fGpffffe670`. So a tuned-up car earns more at race start; the `+0x26` field is the race record's base. Not ported on purpose (online wager feature, see the resolution below); the port pays nothing at race start. **Data read (2026-10-22):** the bracket table at `0x00516530` is eleven {rating, value} pairs `(0,0) (1,500) (2,1000) ... (10,5000)`, so `rating = min(10, total part value / 500)` with linear interpolation (0..10 stars, 500 per star; `DAT_00516580 = 10` is the cap); `DAT_00562ed4 = 0.005`; the area record's s16 at `+4` is 0 for areas 0 and 1 and 100 for areas 2-5 (6 records of 0x50 bytes; `+2` is 100 / 250 / 400, `+6` is 136), so the payment is `int(rating * 100 + 0.005) + race[+0x26]` = up to 1000 in the later areas and 0 in the first two. The payment only runs when the race settings record's `+0x24` is 3 or 4 (`0x0026ce58`; the mode byte, not the 0x34a11 event, whose `+0x24` / `+0x26` are always 0 in all 224 records), and the record `+0x26` is not a field of the event table, so which race modes those are and what `+0x26` holds (a stake?) was unknown. **Resolved (2026-10-22): this payment is an ONLINE-lobby feature, not part of the offline career.** All the callers (`0x001fc970`, `0x001fca90`, `0x001fcc30`, `0x001fcce8`, `0x001fcdd0`, `0x001fd210`, `0x001fe730`, `0x002ce390`) are the UI_OLCarSelect-family screens (class `0x001f2c40`, shared with the Quick Race car select) and every use of the record `DAT_004f0e44` is gated on `DAT_004f0e38 != 0` (the online player / lobby struct); `+0x24` is the online game mode (1 and 2 set `uGpffffe6a8` = 1 / 2, 3 and 4 are the money-wager modes) and `+0x26` the wager stake. The native build has no online mode (the main menu refuses Online, #183), so there is nothing to port: the race-start payment stays out on purpose. The part value of slot `s` is `0x00270288(0x00270888(slot, part), level)`, the same price path as `PartCatalog::Price` (#184), with the paint slot `0x3f` zeroed for a part that does not belong to the car. |

Not ported: and the two curve fields (23 / 24, the Graph screen), the original's slider bar graphics (the rows show label / value / end labels only), the Tip popup, the units option default (the port starts in MPH, so the lowering shows inches), and the single-part flags of `0x0031b720` / `0x0031b8e8`. Flow: Chart Next -> Results; Results Back -> Chart, Next -> Dyno Shop. Dev aid: `NFSU2_TUNE_TEST=1` gives the screens an in-memory Skyline. **Rating bars added (2026-10-23, `Frontend/DynoRatings`):** the Acceleration / Top Speed / Handling bars (`0x0031ce90` / `0x0031cdd8` / `0x0031d060`) normalise frame values with the 30 limit floats of GLOBALB container `0x80034a30` chunk `0x34a32` (copied to `0x0051da68`; Handling is exact: nine weighted ratios, weights at `0x004807a8`). Acceleration and Top Speed need the acceleration-vs-speed curve of `0x0031e0d0` / `0x0031ef20` (best-gear drive acceleration every 3 mph, shift points where the next gear's torque wins); the port rebuilds it from its own drivetrain figures, drag = v^2 * aero / mass, and leaves out the nitrous term of `0x0031ce90` (a car-object field, not in the frame). Stock Skyline: 0.26 / 0.28 / 0.51; the bar track geometry (80.5 left of the backing's centre, 163.5 long) was measured from the layout. | 217 | 2026-10-23 | Physics/Race | NOTE | **In-race nitrous (NOS): decode so far, not ported.** The vehicle model has no nitrous or turbo boost yet (`UG2Vehicle.h`). Engine torque `0x0030c7a0`: `curve(rpm) * throttle`, plus, when the engine's nitrous level (engine `+0x84`, set by `0x0030c798` from `0x00317ac0`) is > 0, `mult[level-1] * frame+0x2ac * nitrousCurve(rpm * DAT_005634c0) * throttle` with `mult` = `{1.0, 1.4, 1.7}` (`0x00480700`); the same term appears per wheel in `0x00314390`. `0x00317ac0` (Vehicle per-frame driver) takes the level from `0x00305150(nitrous system)`: 0 while idle, else 1..3 from the tank fraction `0x00305400` against the thresholds at `0x004806f0` (`{0, 1.4, 1.7}`). The nitrous system object (class around `0x00304080..0x00305f70`): `+0x42c` capacity, `+0x434` current fill (both x 4000 units; `0x003048d0` seeds them from frame `+0x2a0`, the tuning duration, and `+0x2a4`), `+0x438` the activation stamp (0 = off; compared with the game clock `DAT_005683f8` / `DAT_005683c8` in network games), `0x003052c0` starts a burst, `0x00305328` ends it and writes back the remaining fill, `0x003054a8(amount, sys)` refills / drains the tank by a fraction (called from gameplay: drift, near misses, speed; callers not traced). Still to find: the pad button -> `0x003052c0` path (player controller), the refill sources, the HUD NOS bar in `HUD_SingleRace`, the NOS sound banks (#127) and the post-race effect. **Ported (2026-10-23, stand-ins marked):** `UG2Physics::Vehicle` now has the tank (capacity = record `+0x3b8` seconds, starts full; drains 1 s/s while a burst runs; `AddNitrous` = `0x003054a8` clamped to 0..2 of capacity), the level from the fraction (`> 0` / `> 1.4` / `> 1.7`) and the engine torque term of `0x0030c7a0` (`{1.0, 1.4, 1.7}` x record `+0x3c4` x the record's second 9-point curve at `+0x33c` x throttle; ASSUMED to be the engine's `+0x60` curve, it was already loaded as `turbo[]`). The button is `0x0035e868` cases `0xa3` / `0xa4` (press starts via `0x003052c0` if the tank has anything, release ends via `0x00305328` and keeps the rest); the port maps it to Space/Accept held (Q in drag races, where Accept shifts) and only while racing. The HUD's N2O arc (`0x11495cd6` line + `0x11461255` fill, `RaceSession`) is shown for cars with a tank and the fill is cropped from the left to the fraction (the original animates it by script). `EngineSound::NitrousBurst` plays sound 0 of `SOUND\NOS\Nitrous_00_MB.abk` (6 one-shots of 0.16-1.6 s; which is which is assumed). **Refill decoded (2026-10-23):** `0x0036a9e8` runs once a second: `0x00346818` takes the change of race progress (car `+0x918` x 100 = percent of a lap; circuits wrap at +-50) and `0x003054a8` adds `max(delta, 0) * 0.01 * 0.5` of the tank (the factor is 0 in the modes flagged by `DAT_0053e440` / `iGp1d0`, taken here as drag races); with a drift object present (drift races) it adds the drift score gained x 0.000667 instead. The port does exactly that from `RaceSession::PlayerDistance / LapLength` and `DriftTotal`; the mode flags are a guess, and the whole branch only runs when `DAT_0053e468` is set (not traced, assumed on). A 1869 m lap therefore refills half a tank, and the tank can overfill to 2.0 (level 2 above 1.4). Stock Skyline has no tank (capacity 0); a fully tuned one has 6 s / blend 0.5 (Dyno test aid `NFSU2_TUNE_TEST=1 NFSU2_TUNE_PARTS=1`). Dev aids: `NFSU2_NOS_TEST=<s>,<blend>` forces a tank, `NFSU2_NOS_AUTO=1` holds the button, `NFSU2_NOS_FORCE=<0..1>` pins the HUD fill, `NFSU2_NOS_DEBUG=1`. Forced-tank straight-line run: about 8% more speed at 3 s than without. Not done: the turbo boost, purge/blow-off sounds, the post-race effect, the wheel/exhaust visual. | `docs/physics.md`, `Physics/UG2Vehicle.cpp` |
| 218 | 2026-10-23 | Frontend | WORKAROUND | **Car-select stat bars use the real rating formulas.** `FENativeInterface::UpdateStatBars` now feeds the tuned record (stock or with the fitted parts / tune) to `DynoRatings::Compute` (#215: `0x0031ce90` / `0x0031cdd8` / `0x0031d060` with the GLOBALB `0x34a32` limits), so the bars agree with the Dyno Results screen (stock Skyline 0.24 / 0.26 / 0.50); the old 0-100 km/h / top-speed / grip estimate stays as the fallback when the limits are missing. The acceleration and top-speed terms still rebuild the acceleration curve from the port's drivetrain (see #215). | `Frontend/FENative.cpp` |
| 219 | 2026-10-23 | Physics/Race | WORKAROUND | **Car-to-car contacts are rigid-body impulses (stand-in for the original's contact solver).** The old circle push (`main.cpp`) could not move the UG2 player at all: `PlayerCar::Nudge` / `ScaleSpeed` only changed the stored pose that `Update` rewrites from the body each frame; both now act on the body too. New `World/CarCollision`: each car is a rectangle in the ground plane (UG2: wheel hardpoints + 0.95 m overhang / +0.2 m width via `Vehicle::Footprint`; arcade opponents and traffic: 4.4 x 1.9 m, 1.4 t, a point mass that can only change speed along its heading), tested with the separating-axis method; the smallest overlap gives the normal, the deepest corner the contact point; the cars are separated in proportion to inverse mass and answered with an impulse (restitution 0.2, `Vehicle::ApplyImpulse` / `InverseMassAlong` / `VelocityAt` on the body's momentum and inertia) plus Coulomb friction 0.3 for rigid bodies. Two arcade cars still use the old rub fallback. The original path (CarCollisionBody / witness tables, `0x00334110` is the track-polygon contact accumulator, the SIMD contact solver) is NOT decoded; no damage, sparks or collision sound yet. Dev aids: `NFSU2_COLLISION_TEST=1` parks the first opponent 45 m ahead (hit it with `NFSU2_PHYS_TEST=1`), `NFSU2_COLLISION_DEBUG=1` logs closing speeds. Verified: a 33.8 m/s hit on the parked car gives 19 m/s closing, rebound and spin; a 4012 autodrive race stays stable with light contacts (closing <= 3 m/s). | `World/CarCollision.cpp`, `Physics/UG2Vehicle.cpp`, `main.cpp` |
| 220 | 2026-10-23 | Traffic | NOTE | **UG2 traffic AI: structure found, not ported.** The class-name strings (`AIActionDriveTrafficLane`, `AIActionAvoidAvoidableAsTraffic`, `AIActionObeyTrafficIntersections`, `TrafficMover`, `TrafficController`, `TrafficIntersection`, `TrafficTeleporter`) have no code xrefs; their state-serialisation functions (which pass the name) locate the classes: DriveTrafficLane save `0x0010e120` (class code `0x0010d...-0x0010fca8`, `CalculatePointsToDrive` candidates `0x0010e7b8` / `0x0010d978` / `0x0010fca8`), Avoid `0x0010f7a0`, ObeyIntersections `0x00110248`, TrafficMover save `0x00123960`, **TrafficController save `0x00124000`** (class code `0x00124...-0x00126...`; its per-frame spawner is `0x00126218`: a countdown timer, spawns 1 car per tick (16 when a drag/free-roam flag is set) from the streaming list `DAT_00569598` through `0x00127398`), TrafficIntersection save `0x001250b8` (mode word: 1 free, 2 signalled by phase (`+6`), 3 all-go, 4 blocked; 16 x 0x10 approach entries, `0x00125468` waits 3 s before releasing a stopped approach). **TrafficMover::Move = `0x001239e8`** (decoded): a kinematic bicycle model, not the rigid body: wheelbase from the vehicle, steering input -> turn radius (straight line when |steer * wheelbase| is small), heading and (x, y) updated along the arc, speed += dt * (accel * accelCurve(speed) - decel * decelCurve(speed)) with the curves at `0x499390` / `0x4993b8`, clamped to 0..max; the vehicle's pose, wheel spin (speed / wheel radius) and yaw rate are written back for the sounds and tyre effects. The lane action (`0x0010e7b8`) sets throttle (`+0x1fc`), brake (`+0x204`), target speed (`+0x1f0`, <= 20 m/s, 2 x the lane's value when a car blocks), steering (`+500`) from the path points of the AI road network (`FUN_0037c540` / `0x00117fc0` / `0x00118c78`: path infrastructure, not decoded). **Lane data (`PathsFreeRoam.bin` chunk `0x3414d`, L4RG):** the 473 links are 11 separate roads (the u32 at +20 is the road id, 11 distinct values, up to 105 links each), almost all chains (440 of 473 end where exactly one link starts), so the port's geometric `next` already follows them; flags[1] is not a branch count (461 links = 1) - the 12 links with 2..4 belong to road `32653960` and list other road ids in the three pad words (connectors between roads). Intersections are chunk `0x3414a` (16 x 100-byte records). Still to do for a faithful port: the AI road-network structures, the path-following steering (`0x00118c78`), intersection signalling phases and the controller's emitter / teleporter rules. The port's `World/Traffic` (random cruise 11-19 m/s, pure-pursuit steering, slowing for junctions and cars in front) stays. | `World/Traffic.cpp` |
| 221 | 2026-10-23 | Sound | WORKAROUND | **Collision sounds.** The impact sounds are `SOUND\IG_GLOBAL\MB_Stich_Collision.abk` (516 KB, found by hashing the executable's `MB_Stich_Collision.abk` against the ZDIR; an EA ABKC bank of 40+ SPU sounds of 0.03-1.2 s at 18-32 kHz, EA "stitch" collision sets). Which sound belongs to which impact type (metal, glass, scrape, car vs wall vs car) lives in the EAX collision state manager (`CSTATE_Collision`, `SFXObj_Collision`) and is not decoded; `EngineSound::Crash` picks a random sound by length band from the closing speed (< 4 m/s short 0.05-0.3 s, < 9 m/s 0.3-0.55 s, harder 0.55-1.3 s) and `main.cpp` plays it for the player's car-to-car contacts above 1.5 m/s with a 350 ms gap. Wall hits (added later): `Vehicle::CollideWorld` records the closing speed of the hardest wall hit (`TakeWallImpact`, momentum into the wall / mass); above 2 m/s it plays the same banded sound with the same 350 ms gap (a straight 33 m/s run logs a 31 m/s hit). No per-voice volume and no scrape loop yet. Not audible-tested (headless run only): the 33.8 m/s test hit logs closing speeds and plays without error. | `Sound/EngineSound.cpp`, `main.cpp` |

(3) The atlas is a **planar side projection**, not the body's own UVs (those spread diagonally over the atlas): the left side's profile fills the top strip (ground at the top edge, rows 0..85) and the right side's the bottom strip (ground at row ~385 = v 0.752, rows 300..385), car length maps to u 0.043..0.925 and height uses the same scale (the wheel-arch cut-outs are visible in the strips). (5) The painted body is the material slot with the most triangles that spans the car length (`FENativeInterface::BodySlot`, 0x3c84d757 on the Skyline; the shipped slot name is unknown, and the `<CAR>_SKIN1` slot only holds trim edges / a coplanar shell); `ModelViewGL` draws those triangles textured with a depth offset over the coplanar trim shell. Pitfall found: SDL's GL renderer leaves `GL_UNPACK_ROW_LENGTH` etc. set, which scrambled the uploaded skin until they are reset before `glTexImage2D`. Dev aids: `NFSU2_VINYL_TEST="WILD_172B=ffe000,000000,00c0ff;FLAME_128A=..."` (design = `<CATEGORY>_<NUM><LETTER>`, colours red,green,blue regions as hex RGB), `NFSU2_SKIN_DUMP=<file.ppm>`, `--tpk <file> [hash out.ppm]` lists / dumps a texture pack. **Race car + save (later the same day):** `TrackWorld::SetCarSkin` gives a car a composed skin (GL texture rebuilt on replace) and `SetCarTransform` maps the body-slot triangles with the shared `CarSkin::Projection`; `main.cpp` puts the career car's layers (or `NFSU2_VINYL_TEST`) on the player's race car (`NFSU2_CAM_ORBIT=80` shows the side). `OwnedCar::vinyls` (<= 4 layers: design name + 3 region colours) is saved (profile v15, round-trip tested). | 234 | 2026-10-23 | Traffic | WORKAROUND | **Traffic obeys signals at junctions (stand-in).** `TrafficNet` keeps each junction's direction vector (record +0x0c/+0x10, unit). `Traffic::Update` runs a signal clock: every junction gives the cars along its direction axis the green for 9 s, then the cross axis (phase offset by junction index); a car heading at a junction within 30 m on a red axis brakes to stop 8 m short, and cars already inside 7 m go through. The original's `TrafficIntersection` modes (free / phase / all-go / blocked, `0x00125468` 3 s release) are not ported, so the phase length and axis rule are stand-ins. Verified on L4RG (`NFSU2_TRAFFIC=16 NFSU2_TRAFFIC_DEBUG=1`): 1-2 of 16 cars stopped at any time, released within a phase. | `World/Traffic.*`, `World/TrafficNet.*` |
| 235 | 2026-10-23 | Traffic | FIXED | **Traffic keeps a gap to other traffic.** The following check in `Traffic::Update` only looked at the player and rivals; each traffic car now also slows for any other traffic car 7-22 m ahead within 3 m sideways (same rule), so lane chains no longer stack cars through each other. L4RG, 16 cars, 40 s: no lock-ups (0-1 stopped). | `World/Traffic.cpp` |
| 236 | 2026-10-23 | Traffic | NOTE | **Why the original lane action is not ported further.** `0x0010e7b8` (DriveTrafficLane) reads its path point and the lane's limits from the AI road-network structures (`0x0037c540` path lookup, `0x00117fc0` / `0x00118c78` -> `0x00119140` / `0x00118e30` steering helpers, `0x0037ba40` / `0x0037b908` end-of-path tests) and only writes throttle `+0x1fc`, brake `+0x204`, target speed `+0x1f0` (<= 20 m/s from the vehicle's own limit, doubled when the lane is blocked), steering `+0x1f4` from the lane's curvature (`fVar12 + slope*s + k*s*s/2 > 3.0` decides a hard turn). The port's `Traffic` already produces the same outputs (cruise speed <= 19 m/s, pure-pursuit steering, slowing for bends, cars ahead and now signals #234 / gaps #235), and the structures behind `0x0037c540` carry no data beyond the lane links the port loads. A faithful port would reproduce behaviour the port already shows, so it is deliberately not done. | `World/Traffic.cpp` |

| 246 | 2026-10-23 | Career | WORKAROUND | **Visual Rating and DVD covers decoded; stage gating restored.** `0x00267e48` -> `0x002c8fc8`: the Visual Rating of a car is the **sum of the shop prices** (`0x0026b110` = the part's price at its tier, i.e. `PartCatalog::Price` / `PaintCatalog::Price`) of every installed visual part (slot ids 0..0xa9; the wide-body kit makes the bumpers / skirt count for nothing; ids `0x4e..0x5c` and `0x1e` are skipped), mapped linearly by the table at `0x00516530` - 0 points = 0 stars, **one star per $500, ten stars at $5000**. GLOBALB chunk `0x34a16` (30 records x 0x40) are the **media records**: the 10 flag-1 records are DVD covers (`STAGE_2_DVD_1` .. `STAGE_5_DVD_4`, stage `+0x25`, completion id `+0x20`, **star threshold `+0x3c` = 1..10**: cover n needs n stars), the 20 flag-0 records magazine pictures (threshold 0). `0x0026ac88` unlocks (state 2) a cover of the current stage when the rating reaches its threshold, and `0x002666c0` / `0x00266618` enter a stage only when its gate event is done **and every DVD cover of that stage is won** - the "Build a car featured on N DVD covers" contract (#152 left this out as "no customisation system"). Port: `Career::EventTable` parses the media records (`Media()`), `StageCleared` now also requires the stage's DVD ids in the completed list, and `FEStartupSequence::VisualRating` / `UpdateDvdUnlocks` (checked every 1.5 s) sums the fitted body parts, paints, vinyls, decals, rims, neon and tint (the last three at the port's own prices), adds a cover's id to the completed-event list when the stage is reachable and the rating is enough, and grants the covers of a stage whose gate event is already done (old saves keep their progress). Career Status shows the real rating on its star strip (lit stars = rating; the widget's cyan stars are `0x6ec2f0de + n`), `DVD Covers Won n/10` and the DVD page lists the covers of the current stage with their thresholds. Not done: DVD cover artwork / the featured-car display, magazine pictures (no magazine rewards), the exact set of slot ids the original sums for items the port does not have (e.g. spinners, interior). | `Career/Events.*`, `Frontend/FEStartup.*` |
| 247 | 2026-10-23 | Career | NOTE | **The free-roam career layer is in GLOBALB `0x34a1d` (10,492 bytes, stage script strings) and is not ported.** Its string table lists: shop hot spots (`CC_/UC_/IND_CAR_LOT`, `CRIB`, `CC_PERF_SHOP`, `*_BODYSHOP`, `*_GRAPHICS`, `*_CAR_SPECIALTY`, `THE_UNIQUES_SHOP`), the tutorial events (`DDAY_EVENT_A/B`, `STAGE_0/1_*`, `DRAGTUT`, `STREETXTUT`), **SMS text messages** (`SMS_UNLOCK_01..66`, `SMS_LOC_GO_TO_CAR_LOT_NOW`, `SMS_LOC_GO_TO_GARAGE`, `SMS_MAG_*`, `SMS_DVD_COVER_1..10`, `SMS_CAR_UNLOCK_1..17`, `SMS_UNIQUE_*`), loading **ENGAGE_TIPS_*** hints, and per stage the **`STAGE_n_SHOW_k` car-show / magazine locations with `TRIGGER_START_STAGE_n_SHOW_k`** world triggers, the **`STAGE_n_VISUAL_k` / `STAGE_n_PERF_k` unique-part events** (reward `SMS_UNIQUE_STAGE_n_VISUAL_k` / `_PERF_k`, items in the Uniques shop) and every region's event list (`S<n>_CIRCUIT_<m>` / `_SPRINT` / `_DRAG` / `_DRIFT` / `_STREET` / `_URL` / `_SUV` / `_SPON_*` / `_H_*` with `EV_<nn>` locators, the sponsor names Bride, Alpine, OZ Wheels, Nitrous Express, Kenwood, Sparco, Enkei, Brembo, Konig, Bilstein, Stillen, NeuSpeed, Greddy, ...). So: magazine pictures are won by driving to / triggering the numbered car-show locations in free roam (not by a menu or a race), the Uniques shop is fed by the VISUAL / PERF unique events, and DVD covers / car unlocks arrive as SMS messages. The port has none of the free-roam trigger / SMS layer (the crib stands in for the garage and the world map for the event locators). Porting it needs the trigger positions (not found in this chunk; the `EV_nn` / `TRIGGER_*` names are looked up in the region's scene / path data) and the SMS UI (`UI_...` SMS screens were not located). Recommended order if continued: (1) SMS messages for the already-decoded unlock moments (DVD cover won, stage opened), (2) the show trigger positions from the region data, (3) the Uniques shop. | `ISSUES.md` |

| 249 | 2026-10-23 | Career/Frontend | WORKAROUND | **Career SMS inbox: message data, delivery rules and both screens (continues #247 / #248).** GLOBALB `0x34a17` (parser `0x0026d3d8`) = 167 message definitions of 0x14 bytes `{u16 name offset into the 0x34a1d pool, u8 type +2, u8 category +3, u8 popup +4, u8 icon +5, u32 param +8, u32 param2 +0xc, u32 sender +0x10}`; `0x34a1b` (parser `0x002647b8`) = the 28 triggers of 0x18 bytes `{u16 name, u16 locator, s16 area, u8 kind (0 car show, 2 unique part), u8 value, u32 message-name hash, u32 media id (0x34a16 +0x20), u8 +0x14, u8 +0x15}` (every hash matches a definition name by `bStringHash`). The **type** is the delivery rule (`0x0026d510` / `0x0026d620`): 1 = the finished event's id is `param` or `param2`; 9 / 10 / 12 = the area's (`param >> 16`) sponsor / URL / ordinary events done >= `param & 0xffff` (the three completion lists of the area object: kind +0x34 1 -> 4 slots at +0x8c, 2 -> 10 slots at +0x9c, 3 and not special -> 35 slots, `0x002661f0`); 14 = the DVD cover whose star threshold equals `param` was won (`0x0026ac88` -> `0x0026e5c8`); 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 / 6 / 13 / 15 are sent by name from the tutorial state machines (`0x002720a0` / `0x002cd8a0` / `0x00271e58`), the Outrun code and free-roam triggers. The **category** is the mailbox tab (`0x001f1928`: 1 Inbox, 2 Game Tips, 3 Special Events, 4 Rachel, 5 Unlocks). Event completion (`0x00268a30`) runs `0x0026e530` (the unlock rules over every definition) and `0x0026ee90` (car-show triggers of the area fire when its ordinary-event count equals the trigger's value; the text is `SMS_MAG_GENERIC_BODY/SUBJ_<1|2>` with the media record's `+0x28` language hash as the `%s`, `0x0026dae0`). Kind-2 triggers (`SMS_UNIQUE_*`, unique part events) fire from `0x0036d9e0` only after a race whose type is 6 (the free-roam unique events), so they are decoded but not delivered. Mailbox entries (profile `+0x5d40`, 0x14 bytes, at most 0x100, once per definition): `+0` definition, `+4` read, `+5` popup shown, `+6` deleted, `+7` filed (`0x0026e1e8` files the engage-popup messages by category when the mailbox / dialog closes; unfiled ones show only under Inbox, `0x001f21d8`), `+8` / `+0x10` generic body / subject keys, `+0xc` the `%s` argument. Text: `<name>_SUBJECT` / `<name>_BODY`, except the `SMS_CAR_UNLOCK_n` family which shares `SMS_CAR_UNLOCK_1_*` (`0x0026d718` / `0x0026d820`); the sender (`From: %s`) is the definition's `+0x10` language hash (Rachel, Bayview Car Specialties Shop, ...). **Port:** `Career/Sms.*` (table, delivery, text keys), `Profile::inbox` (save v21), `UI_SMS_Mailbox.fng` (rows `MESSAGE_TEXT_n` / `ENGAGE_PHONE_ICON_n` (tinted per icon type, `0x001f1420`; open envelope `0xee28ac57` when read) / `HIGH_LIGHT_n`, tab title `0x980822b3`, tab arrows `0x54049a3d` / `0xd506a970`; FEng names are hashed upper-case) and `UI_EngageMessageDialog.fng` (`From:` `0xfeced617`, `Subject:` `0x2c167533`, body wrapped into `BODY_TEXT_1..10`); `0x001f1738`'s rule is kept (exactly one unread message -> the dialog, else the mailbox). Input: the package's own mapping delivers Up / Down / Accept (`0x0c407210`) / Back / R1 (`0xd9feec59`) / L1; the pad's square (Delete) and Left / Right come from the pad in `Tick`. **Stand-ins:** the original reaches the mailbox from the free-roam pause menu and an arrival alert, the port has no free-roam pause, so the crib has an **Inbox** option (envelope icon, label `Inbox` `0xc06e138a`); the Delete prompt wording (`0x00260528` -> `0x001f23c75`) is not decoded ("Delete this message?"); the body wrap width (58 characters) is a guess; the timed tutorial messages (type 3: SMS_INSTRUCTION at 10 / 40 / 70 / 100 s), the Outrun and location-trigger messages, the SMS_UNIQUE_* set and the car-unlock messages (type 10 with the Car Lot) are not delivered. The language table really contains the literal `MAGAZINE_SETUP_TUR` for the cover media name `0x601462b5` (the same in the original). Dev aids: `NFSU2_SMS_TEST=<n>` (fills an empty box; 0 = every 3rd definition), `NFSU2_SMS_VIEW=<NAME>` (opens one message), `NFSU2_SMS_DEBUG=1`, `NFSU2_AWARD_TEST` now takes a comma list. Verified: five career wins deliver `SMS_UNLOCK_01` (2nd ordinary event of area 1), `SMS_LOC_X_SPONSOR`, `SMS_UNLOCK_30` at their area counts; one unread opens the dialog from the crib, three the mailbox; tabs, open, back, delete + save, and `tests/profile_test` (v21 round-trip). | `Career/Sms.*`, `Career/Profile.*`, `Career/Events.*` (media name hash), `Frontend/FEStartup.*`, `main.cpp`, `tests/profile_test.cpp` |

(`0x272ebeba`). **Availability** (`0x00268dd0`): every car starts available, then each car listed in GLOBALB `0x34a1f` (24 records of 12 bytes, parsers `0x00264cd0` / lookup `0x00264d38`: `{u32 bStringHash(car folder, upper-case), u32 event id A, u32 event id B}`) is dropped unless its event (B when the region flag `0x002dbae0` is non-zero, which it is on this disc: initial value 1 at `0x00567d8c`) is complete (`0x00268bd8`).
