| 262 | 2026-10-23 | Render | FIXED | **Car shadow flicker.** The contact shadow (#255) was one flat quad at the car's lowest vertex plus 8 cm. That vertex moves every frame with pitch, roll, the polygonal tyre rolling (#257) and steering (#258), and the quad cut in and out of sloped ground. The shadow is now a 6 x 4 grid draped over the **ground**: each grid vertex asks the world's ground query (`GroundHeight`, below the car's origin + 0.6 m; falls back to a low-passed car height where the world has no ground, e.g. the garage room), is smoothed per vertex over frames (a step over 0.4 m snaps), and sits 6 cm above the road with the polygon offset; it fades out as the car leaves the ground (full strength on the road, gone 1.6 m up) and is skipped for cars further than 70 m (keeps the ground queries cheap). Measured driving 14 s on L4RA (`NFSU2_SHADOW_DEBUG=1` prints the old and new height of the player's shadow): mean frame-to-frame step 0.056 m -> 0.0046 m, largest step 0.154 m -> 0.034 m. | `World/TrackWorldGL.cpp`, `World/TrackWorld.h` |
| 263 | 2026-10-23 | Render | FIXED | **Custom GLSL shaders for the world renderer.** The driver gives SDL's GL backend a 4.6 compatibility context (GLSL 4.60), so the renderer now uses GLSL 1.20 programs that keep the fixed-function vertex arrays and matrices (`World/GLShader.*` loads the GL 2.0 / 3.0 entry points through `SDL_GL_GetProcAddress`: shaders, uniforms, generic attributes, framebuffers, `glBlitFramebuffer`; sources in `World/Shaders.h`). **World program:** `texture * vertex colour` (the PS2's modulate), a mild saturation and shadow-lift grade, and smooth distance fog (replaces the fixed-function linear fog; the backdrop panorama draws without fog). **Car program:** per-pixel paint instead of the CPU's per-vertex lighting (#253): smooth normals and the gloss class (0 tyres / lights / badges keep the CPU-lit colour, 1 paint and vinyl skins, 2 glass) travel as generic attributes 1 / 2 of a 40-byte vertex; hemisphere + wrapped sun diffuse, a **clear coat** (Fresnel with F0 0.045 paint / 0.085 glass, a procedural night-city environment reflected through the pixel normal: sodium horizon glow, rows of distant window lights, two soft light boxes, a dark ground), two specular lobes (tight sun + broad sheen), a view-dependent metallic flake, tinted glass that still picks up the surroundings. **Post-processing** (`TrackWorld::PostProcess`, after the world and before SDL draws the HUD): the multisampled window frame is resolved into a texture (`glBlitFramebuffer`), a soft-knee bright pass (threshold 0.62) builds a quarter-size bloom, two separable 5-tap Gaussian iterations blur it, and the composite adds it (`NFSU2_BLOOM=<strength>`, default 0.75), applies a filmic ACES shoulder mixed 55%, a touch of saturation, an exposure of 1.06 and a vignette (0.34). Lit shop windows, neon and lamps now glow and the frame edges fall off. **Switches:** `NFSU2_NO_SHADERS=1` (the old fixed-function path, which stays as the fallback and is used when a driver lacks the entry points or a program fails to link), `NFSU2_NO_POST=1`, `NFSU2_NO_GRADE=1`, `NFSU2_CAR_FLAT=1`; compile / link logs go to stderr. A per-vertex **ambient occlusion** (attribute 3, `aAO`) darkens a rim point by its depth inside the barrel, measured from the outboard lip along the axle. FINDINGS on the Intel Xe iGPU: GPU-bound at ~35 ms (28 fps) with ~6,000 draws and 1.05 M triangles per frame; props + Bauhaus cost only ~3 ms, MSAA ~1.5 ms, post ~1 ms; the world fragment shader cost ~22 ms even with every feature switched off at run time, because one 13-sampler shader with runtime branches makes every pixel pay for the whole shader's registers (a minimal FS gave 13 ms GPU). | 283 | 2026-10-24 | Performance | FIXED | **Draw-call batching, vertex arena, no streaming hitches (28 -> locked 60 fps).** (1) ONE GL vertex arena (`m_arena`, ~500 MB sized from the render lists up front) holds every cell; a VAO captures the fixed-function array state; cells are sub-allocated with `glBufferSubData` (no per-cell buffers). (2) Every visible cell's ranges are grouped into batches by (group, texture, blend mode, window class) and each batch is ONE `glMultiDrawArrays`: **~6,000 draws -> ~440 per frame**, buffer binds 380 -> 0, program switches 200 -> 8; groups: opaque near (full shader), opaque far (lite), window facades (interior program), blended near / far. (3) All cells are uploaded once at world load (`m_warmAll`, one `scratch` buffer reserved for the largest cell) instead of the first time they come into view: 0 uploads per frame in steady state, the CPU copies are freed at once (RSS 1.1 GB -> 0.9 GB), no first-sight hitches. (4) Per-frame heap churn removed from the render and race loops: the pause-menu entry vectors, the rivals / cars / avoid / minimap-dot vectors are static and reused, the cell lists are reused: heap allocations per frame 53 -> ~12 (the rest come from SDL / Mesa / FEng containers). Profiler additions: `NFSU2_ALLOC_TRACE=1` (allocation call-site histogram), `NFSU2_OVERLAY=1`, uploads counted in the overlay. Measured on the Intel Xe iGPU, 1280x720, 4x MSAA, free race on L4RA: CPU world 12 -> 5 ms, GPU 35 -> 12.5 ms, frame 36 -> 16.7 ms (99 % 16.9 ms, worst 17 ms, vsync-locked 60). Also fixed a day-mode regression found with it: additive night "light pool" textures washed roads white (now x0.2 in daylight, `uGlow`) and the street-lamp light cones (beam textures) are dropped by day. NOT done (stated honestly): persistent-mapped / Multi-Draw-Indirect buffers (the world is static, so one-time upload + MultiDraw is already CPU-cheap; MDI would save ~nothing more here and the moving cars are a few thousand triangles), hardware instancing (the props are baked into the arena: zero per-prop draw calls; instancing would only save ~140 MB of vertex memory, not time), a full data-oriented rewrite of the entities, and CPU-side culling of far props. | `World/TrackWorldGL.cpp`, `World/GLBridge.*`, `World/TrackWorld.*`, `Perf.cpp`, `main.cpp` |

| 291 | 2026-10-24 | Physics | WORKAROUND | **Drifting (`Vehicle::UpdateDrift` / `DriftForces`, arcade layer over the faithful UG2 tyre model).** New input: **handbrake = X / Left Shift (pad square)**, `NFSU2_DRIVE` token `hand`. Scoring: points scale with speed and slide angle, the multiplier steps every 1.5 s of unbroken drift (x10), the chain banks after 0.8 s without scoring; the HUD shows an amber DRIFT panel (chain x multiplier, angle bar) and a rising +points popup. FIRST VERSION WAS WRONG and is the lesson: the strong effects ran on every cornering slide and a throttle + lock "power-over", and the autodrive lap test (race 4012) went from 812 m in 45 s to stuck at 230 m, so ordinary driving is now untouched (autodrive again 812.7 m, same as `NFSU2_NO_DRIFT=1`). `NFSU2_NO_DRIFT=1` = faithful tyres only, `NFSU2_NO_POWEROVER=1`, `NFSU2_DRIFT_DEBUG=1` log. | 293 | 2026-10-24 | Physics / Map / Collisions | FIXED | **Accuracy pass: a physics benchmark, the automatic gearbox, footprint wall contact and the world maps.** (1) **`NFSU2_DYNO_BENCH=1`**: every Quick Race car flat out on a bare plane (`TrackWorld::SetFlatGround`, no walls; `NFSU2_BENCH_CAR=<name>` for one car) printing 0-60 mph, 0-100 km/h, 0-100 mph, the quarter mile and top speed next to the dyno model (`PlayerCar::SimulateRun`) and the record's limiter. It found that **most cars were badly wrong**: Focus 20.6 s to 60 mph and 118 km/h at best (limiter 201), Tiburon 36 s, Corsa 27 s, Hummer 29 s; only the Skyline was right. Cause: `Vehicle::AutoGearbox` judged the shift by the engine's own speed, which is still the free-revved ~6500 rpm of the shift that just ended, so after every shift it upshifted again (a Focus went 1-2-3-4-5 at 33 mph, then hunted). It now uses the rpm the driven wheels imply in the current gear. After: every car reaches its limiter (201-256 km/h), 0-60 mph 6.1-12.1 s, quarter mile 14.6-19.2 s; the ~1 s gap to the idealised dyno model is the two clutch cycles. (2) **Wall contact (`CollideWorld`)**: the old stand-in cast three centreline rays and cancelled the normal momentum (the nose could sit metres inside a wall, flanks never touched anything, nothing spun the car). Now eight points of the body rectangle (corners, mid-sides, nose, tail) are swept along their own velocity and a hit applies a proper impulse at that point: restitution 0.22 for hard hits, Coulomb friction 0.35 along the wall, so a glancing hit slides and turns the nose along the wall (yaw 0.35 -> 0.14 in the test). `NFSU2_OLD_WALLS=1` = the old rays. Autodrive lap check unchanged (813 m in 45 s). (3) **Career World Map**: the loaded garage region's road bitmap covers 1 x 0.7 km but the events sit over the 4 km city, so their markers fell off the map (the same tiny circuit for every event). A throwaway load of L4RA's road geometry builds a city map once on first opening (2.6 s; lane-link fallback `Minimap::BuildFromLinks`), with the event markers on it. (4) **In-game World Map** now plots the shops and the garage in their light-column colours (the legend listed them but none were drawn). Dev aids: `NFSU2_STRAIGHT_TEST`, `NFSU2_CITYMAP_TEST`, `NFSU2_WM_DEBUG`, `NFSU2_SHIFT_DEBUG`. | `Physics/UG2Vehicle.*`, `World/TrackWorld.*`, `World/Minimap.*`, `main.cpp` |

| 299 | 2026-10-24 | Graphics / Road + Perf | FIXED | **A worn, weathered road surface (world shader, FULL variant, within 150 m) and a map performance regression.** On top of the photographic asphalt (#274) the shader now adds: broad tonal drift (two noise octaves, +-13 %), darker smoother **repaired patches** with hard edges, **fine wiggly cracks** (Worley cell edges warped by noise, 0.8 m cells, 3 cm wide, only in patchy worn stretches, plus a hairline layer; only within 30-55 m so they never shimmer; the first version used 1.9 m cells and 5 cm lines and read as dried mud or paving slabs, fixed), **oil and rubber stains**, a few bright grains of aggregate, a **sheen**: Fresnel reflection of the sky (the far road turns glossy at a grazing angle, bluish by day, near black at night) scaled by a per-patch roughness plus a sun glint (Blinn-Phong, day only), and **worn yellow lane paint** (uneven chipping and a touch more punch). The sheen fades out between 250 and 520 m. Wet roads at night (#274) are unchanged. **Regression found while measuring**: the modern minimap of #296 / #297 cost 14 ms of CPU per frame (60 -> 34 fps; `NFSU2_PERF=1`, classic map back at 1 ms): `RenderModern` sampled the road bitmap 13 times (bilinear) per pixel for the edge and the glow. Fixed by precomputing the edge and blurred-glow images once in `Minimap::BuildDerived` (3 lookups per pixel), refreshing the map image at 30 Hz (the arrow is drawn live, `Hud::mapNew` skips the texture upload), coarser arc steps (0.09 rad) and fewer shade rings: 59.5-60 fps again with the speed effect, road shader and both HUD parts running. Lesson: run `NFSU2_PERF=1` after every HUD / shader change. NOT done: the wheel tracks (polished lanes where tyres run: the lane direction is not known in the shader), road reflections of buildings and cars (planar or screen-space), tyre marks that persist from the AI cars, puddle ripples. | `World/Shaders.h`, `World/Minimap.*`, `Frontend/ModernUI.*`, `main.cpp` |

| 305 | 2026-10-25 | Graphics | WORKAROUND | **God rays, lens reflections and a sun glint on window glass.** The post pass now keeps the frame's **depth** (a DEPTH24 texture on the resolve framebuffer; `glBlitFramebuffer` with the depth bit; GL errors are drained first; `NFSU2_NO_RAYS=1` or a failed attach disables everything below), so far depth = sky. `TrackWorld::RenderGL` projects the sky shader's sun direction through the frame's camera basis and frustum to a screen position (`m_sunUV`, may lie above the frame) and a strength (`m_raysK`, fades as the sun swings away or behind). `kCompositeFS`: (1) **god rays**: 48 jittered samples from each pixel towards the sun, collecting open sky weighted by the scene's luminance with a 0.962 decay per step, thinned by distance from the sun and by pixel-is-sky, tinted warm and added - buildings, trees and cars cut real shafts out of the sky; (2) **lens reflections** while the sun disc is visible (5 depth taps): five coloured ghosts along the line through the screen centre, a halo ring, a soft core and a blue anamorphic streak, fading as the sun leaves the frame. (3) The interior-mapped window shader (`kInteriorFS`, now with `uDay`) adds the sun mirrored in the glass (`pow(dot(reflect(view, N), sun), 160)`, brighter at glancing angles). Cost about 1.7 ms of GPU time at 1280 x 720 (still 60 fps on the Iris Xe). Dev aids: `NFSU2_RAYS=<k>` (strength; 2 shows the sky mask), `NFSU2_RAYS_SUN=u,v` puts the sun anywhere on screen, `NFSU2_RAYS_DEBUG=1` prints the sun position. Day mode only; the menu stage has none. Limits: the sun (elevation ~40 deg) is above the frame in most street views, so the shafts then come from the top edge through sky gaps; the car's own sun vector is still (0.40,0.30,0.87). |

| 311 | 2026-10-25 | Frontend / Loading | FIXED | **Smooth, stylish screen transitions.** (1) `ModernUI::CaptureFrame` keeps the window's current frame; `DrawWipe(t)` draws it over what lies beneath, cut away by a slanted edge (top leaning right, 0.2 x height) that sweeps left to right with an ease-in-out curve, a slight zoom and fade of the leaving frame, a dark shadow, a blue-and-amber glow and a hot white core. (2) Menu -> loading: the menu frame (captured just before the race request starts the load) wipes away over the loading screen; a load that starts without a capture fades in from black (450 ms). (3) Loading -> game: the finished loading frame (captured when the bar reaches 100 %) wipes away over the first game frames. The wipe advances by at most 33 ms per drawn frame (a hitching first frame cannot swallow it; it measures 0.9 s over 55 frames). (4) The loading screen no longer freezes at 100 %: the first `RenderGL` used to upload every cell in one ~1.3 s step, now `TrackWorld::SetWarmBudget(24)` uploads it in 24 ms slices while `main` redraws the loading screen ("Preparing graphics", bar 85 -> 100 %), and `TrackWorld::Tick` reports progress from the collision, scenery-instance and street-prop loops at most every 33 ms. The animated backdrop therefore plays through the whole load. Switches: `NFSU2_NO_TRANSITIONS=1` (no wipes / fade), `NFSU2_NO_WARMUP=1` (old one-step upload), `NFSU2_TRANS_DEBUG=1` (prints frame count and time of each wipe). | 316 | 2026-10-25 | Physics / Landing, roll | WORKAROUND | **Cars sank through the road after a jump and tumbled; added roll stability.** (1) A new dev aid `NFSU2_LAUNCH="seconds,vz[,vside,vfwd]"` throws the car once. A 9 m/s vertical jump reproduced the bug: the spring force is capped at the car's weight, the car sank until its hardpoints were more than 0.6 m below the road (the ground probe's start), found no ground, fell through and rolled around under it (up.z -0.9). `Vehicle::SuspensionAndContact` now applies a contact impulse (8 % rebound, along the ground normal) at any wheel that has used up its travel while moving into the surface, plus a small positional push-out; nothing is added when moving away, so kerbs cannot launch the car. The same jump now lands and settles at the road height. (2) Roll stability: with three or more wheels down, a restoring torque about the length axis levels the body to the surface normals and the roll rate is damped (`NFSU2_ROLL_K`, default 40); it fades out on the side, so a real crash still rolls. A pure sideways slide of 14 and 30 m/s did not tip the car even without it, so the drift roll the user saw was not reproduced; this is a safety net, not a confirmed root-cause fix. |

The reward manager is the profile object at `0x4e9310` (`+8` = current record, `+0x11c` state 1 event running (timer at `+4`) / 3 event matched (`0x0026f1e0`), finalised to 5 by `0x0026f0d8` / 4 choose reward (`0x0026f098`) / 5 done / 6 failed (`0x0026f310`) / 7-8 reset or restart, list of up to 0x40 parts at `+0xc`, count `+0x110`, category `+0x114`); the list builder is `0x0026f5d0` (mode 1 category `>` = 0x3e picks tier-4 `0x18` parts; `\r` = 0x1d body parts of tier 4, one per name keeping the highest finish/level; otherwise `GetPartList(car, cat, tier 4)`), run from the post-win handler `0x00268770` via `0x0026f098`. The screen is a part browser over the shop session `0x514550` (class `0x002b0bf0`, vtable `0x477e10`, Act `0x002b10c8`). So the Uniques ARE the tier-4 (level 4, price 0) parts of PartCatalog for the VISUAL (mode 1) uniques - tools/carparts.py shows the Skyline has exactly 10 level-4 records (id 6 VAPOR/MANTIS/ARMAGEDDON/APOCALYPSE, id 9 SPEEDLINE/FACTOR X/TRAP + carbon). CORRECTION: the numbers in the mode-2 sets (1/5/7, 2/9/8, 4/6/3) are PERFORMANCE category numbers, not part ids; that 6 and 9 also occur is a coincidence. Trigger mapping (area + value is the unlock threshold, not an index): mode 1 = the five `STAGE_n_VISUAL_k` spots (rec 2 VISUAL_1 cat 0x0b, rec 8 VISUAL_2 cat 0x0d, rec 15/16 VISUAL_3/4 cats 0x0c/0x3e, rec 27 VISUAL_5 cat 0x16), mode 2 = the three `PERF_k` spots (rec 7 PERF_1, rec 17 PERF_2, rec 26 PERF_3); the VISUAL_3 vs _4 order of recs 15/16 is a guess. **Category maps (decoded):** visual category -> part id via `0x00270b40`: 0x0b->9 (hood: SPEEDLINE/FACTOR X/TRAP), 0x0c->0x0c (spoiler), 0x0d->0x1d (body-kit finish set), 0x16->6 (wide-body kits KITW: VAPOR/MANTIS/ARMAGEDDON/APOCALYPSE; the port's shop also treats part id 6 as the wide body), 0x3e->the `0x18` tier-4 branch of `0x0026f5d0`; performance category (1..10) -> shop slot via `0x00270c88`: 1->3, 2->7, 3->5, 4->2, 5->1, 6->0, 7->8, 8->6, 9->4, 10->9, so the three PERF spots offer slots {3,1,8}, {7,4,6}, {2,0,5} (a slot is only offered if the unlock array at `+0x2f4` of the object `0x00268da0(0x4e8710)` has it clear, `0x00277b50`). Select in the screen (`0x002b0c40`, event 0xd05fc3a3): visual = `0x002b26d0` fits the part on the preview ride + `0x0026b038` adds it to the car and its price to the value total; performance = `0x002b2b08` sets the per-slot flag at session `+0xc68 + slot*4` (no code reads `+0xc68` / `+0x378` directly: they are ride `+0x318 + slot*4` of the preview / real ride, and the ride region `+0x2f0` is the installed performance-package set (`0x0031a608` installs a package part by id, `0x0031a8f0(set, levels, category)` installs levels 1..n of a category, `0x0038f038` turns the set into attribute levels); after the flag is set the screen calls `0x0038fef0(ride, 1, ...)` = `0x0038f038(ride, 1, 0)` + `0x0031d570`, i.e. a full re-evaluation of the car's performance, so the flag is an input there; the exact formula is unresolved). After either Select, `0x0026f138` sets the manager state to 5 and `0x0026f4e0` marks the reward's career object (`0x00265108(0x4e8710, hash(name), 1)` = claimed), which is what stops it being offered again; both play the sound cue 8/9 (`0x002ce7c8` / `0x002ce810`). Next: deliver the reward when the PERF_k/VISUAL_k locator is won, list tier-4 parts of the category via PartCatalog, reuse the generic parts browser to pick and fit one. |

| 324 | 2026-10-28 | Career / Physics / RE | NOTE | **What a performance Unique does (closes the open question of #319).** The slot flags at ride `+0x318 + slot*4` are the integer part of the installed-performance set (`ride+0x2f0`: floats 0..9 = package levels read by `0x0031a4e0`, then these flags at set `+0x28..+0x4c`, then the installed-part bytes `+0x50 + part id`). `0x0031b720(carRec+0x110, physics, set)` applies the CAR'S OWN bonus tables when a slot flag is set (values from the 0x890-byte car record, file offset = game offset + 8, i.e. record frame `+0x118` as in PerfModel): **slot 0** `mass *= (1 - rec+0x2b0)` (Skyline 0.015, weight reduction), **slot 1** `phys+0x1c0 = rec+0x2b4` (Skyline 0.6, Civic 0.199), **slot 2** `phys+0x2ac += rec+0x2b8` (0.125), **slot 3** `phys[0x200+i] += rec+0x2d0+i` (8 floats: Skyline 0.015 0.017 0.017 0.016 0.016 0.015 0.013 0.011 - a torque-curve bonus), **slot 4** `phys[0x200+i] += rec+0x318+i` and `phys[0x224+i] += rec+0x318+i` (0, 0, .003 .. .012), **slot 6** `phys+0x274 += rec+0x2bc` (0.06), **slot 7** `phys[0x200+i] += rec+0x2f4+i` (0.015 .. 0.009), **slot 8** per axle `phys[0xdc/0xe0 (stride 8)] += rec+0x2c0/0x2c8` (0.05 each). Slot 5 is not used by this function. The three PERF spots offer slots {3,1,8} (PERF_1), {7,4,6} (PERF_2) and {2,0,5} (PERF_3) through `0x00270c88` (category 1->3, 2->7, 3->5, 4->2, 5->1, 6->0, 7->8, 8->6, 9->4, 10->9); choosing a slot sets that flag and the car's performance is recomputed (`0x0038fef0`). So a performance Unique is a per-car, per-slot bonus (torque curve for 3/4/7, weight for 0, ...) that the shop's packages do not give. NOT ported: `PerfModel` does not read these record fields (`grep` finds no use of `+0x2b0..+0x33c`), and the physics fields `phys+0x1c0 / 0x274 / 0x2ac / 0xdc` still need to be named (the engine torque block is `0x0031d2e0` / `0x0031d438`). |
| 325 | 2026-10-28 | Career / Uniques / Physics | FIXED | **Performance Uniques (`PERF_1..3`) work: pick one of three slots, the car's own bonus is applied.** `PerfModel::Apply` now ports the slot flags of `0x0031b720` (#324): weight x (1 - rec+0x2b0), `phys+0x1c0 = rec+0x2b4`, nitrous +0x2ac += rec+0x2b8, tyre +0x274 += rec+0x2bc, per-axle +0xdc/+0xe0 += rec+0x2c0/0x2c8, and the nine torque-curve points += rec+0x2d0 (slot 3) / rec+0x2f4 (slot 7) / rec+0x318 (slot 4, also into +0x224). A won Unique is stored as a marker part id `0xf000 + slot` in `OwnedCar::perfParts` (`PerfModel::kUniqueBase`), so it saves with the car and flows through every existing `TunedCarRecord` / tuning-session path unchanged (package lookups ignore the unknown id). The spot's three reward categories (`SmsTrigger::part / part2 / part3`, bytes +0x15..0x17) map to slots through `0x00270c88` and are labelled with the shop's own category names (Engine, Transmission, Tires, ...); a slot already won is not offered again. Tested on the scratch save: `PERF_1` listed Engine / Transmission / Tires, Accept on Engine (slot 3) fitted it and the Skyline's peak torque went 299.0 -> 311.9 Nm; `--test` still passes. All seven placed Unique spots of L4RA (3 PERF + 4 VISUAL) are now offered. Not checked: the other slots' effect in a drive, the original's 3D preview and price total. |
